  1. (a) Noise power: \(N=-174+10\log_{10}(5\times10^6)=-174+66.99=-107.01\) dBm.
  2. SNR = −90 − (−107.01) = 17.01 dB = 50.2.
    C = 5×10⁶ × log₂(1 + 50.2) = 5×10⁶ × 5.68 = 28.4 Mbps
  3. (b) Doubling the bandwidth doubles the noise power (3 dB more): N = −104.0 dBm, SNR = 14.0 dB = 25.1.
    C = 10×10⁶ × log₂(1 + 25.1) = 10×10⁶ × 4.71 = 47.1 Mbps
  4. The capacity grows only by about 1.66 times, not 2: the bandwidth doubles but the SNR halves, so the log term gets smaller.
  5. (c) Assume both subchannels get power: \(\dfrac{2}{\gamma_0}=1+\dfrac1{20}+\dfrac1{0.5}=3.05\ \Rightarrow\ \gamma_0=0.656\). Subchannel 2 has SNR 0.5 < 0.656, so it should get no power; drop it and recompute.
  6. Only subchannel 1 left: \(\dfrac1{\gamma_0}=1+\dfrac1{20}=1.05\), \(\gamma_0=0.952\); \(P_1/P=1/\gamma_0-1/20=1\), all the power goes to subchannel 1.
    C = 1 MHz × log₂(20 / 0.952) = 1 MHz × log₂21 = 4.39 Mbps
(a) SNR ≈ 17 dB (50.2), C ≈ 28.4 Mbps; (b) C ≈ 47.1 Mbps, about 1.66 times, not double; (c) subchannel 2 gets no power, P₁ = P, C ≈ 4.39 Mbps
Official answer: none (self-written mock question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
For comparison, splitting the power equally (P/2 each, SNRs 10 and 0.25) gives C = log₂11 + log₂1.25 = 3.78 Mbps, 0.61 Mbps less than water-filling. Power given to a very poor subchannel buys almost no capacity.
Step by step: what water-filling is and why it allocates power this way

QuestionWhat water-filling answers

Given several parallel subchannels of different quality and a fixed total power, how should the power be split to maximise the total capacity? The transmitter must know the channel (informed transmitter).

AnalogyPouring water into an uneven pool

Each subchannel is one cell of a pool whose floor height is 1/γj (γj is its SNR with all the power): the better the channel, the lower the floor. Pour the total power in like water; it settles to a flat surface at 1/γ₀. Each cell gets power = surface − floor: good channels get more, poor ones less, and a cell whose floor is above the surface gets none.

\[\frac{P_j}{P}=\frac{1}{\gamma_0}-\frac{1}{\gamma_j}\ (\gamma_j\ge\gamma_0),\qquad \sum_j\Big(\frac{1}{\gamma_0}-\frac{1}{\gamma_j}\Big)=1,\qquad C=\sum_{\gamma_j\ge\gamma_0}B_j\log_2\frac{\gamma_j}{\gamma_0}\]

StepsAssume all on, check, drop and redo if needed

  1. Assume all n subchannels get power: \(n/\gamma_0=1+\sum1/\gamma_j\); solve for γ₀.
  2. Check every \(\gamma_j\ge\gamma_0\); if one fails, drop the worst (n − 1) and solve again.
  3. Compute the powers and the capacity.

HereSubchannel 2 is dropped

Assume both on: 2/γ₀ = 1 + 1/20 + 1/0.5 = 3.05, γ₀ = 0.656; subchannel 2 has SNR 0.5 < 0.656 (its floor 1/0.5 = 2 is above the surface 1/0.656 = 1.52), so drop it. One left: 1/γ₀ = 1.05, P₁/P = 1.05 − 0.05 = 1, C = log₂(20/0.952) = log₂21 = 4.39 Mbps. An equal split gives only log₂11 + log₂1.25 = 3.78 Mbps.

Compare Practice 5 Q1 (SNRs 80, 40, 40): γ₀ = 2.82, all three are above it and all get power (0.342, 0.329, 0.329), C ≈ 124.7 Mbps.

WhyThis split is optimal

Capacity is log₂(1 + SNR), which grows more and more slowly. Extra power on a poor channel barely raises its SNR; a good channel returns more per unit of power, but with diminishing returns too. At the optimum the surface is flat: the last bit of power earns the same on every channel used (the slides say γ₀ comes from a Lagrange multiplier; the formal derivation is not examined).

  • Common mistakes: use linear γj; always check γj ≥ γ₀ or you get a negative power; capacity uses log₂(γj/γ₀), not log₂(1 + γj); update n after dropping a subchannel.
In one line: treat 1/γj as the pool floor and pour in the total power to a flat surface 1/γ₀; good channels get more, poor ones less, very poor ones none. Assume all on → solve γ₀ → check γj ≥ γ₀ → drop and redo if needed.
◆ Key points
What it tests
Shannon capacity (memorise), noise power N₀B, how capacity depends on bandwidth, water-filling (Ch 2 p.48, p.54–57).
Background
C = B log₂(1 + SNR) with linear SNR; N = N₀B, in dB N₀ + 10 log₁₀B; water-filling: Σ(1/γ₀ − 1/γj) = 1, C = ΣBj log₂(γj/γ₀).
Method
(a)(b) Noise → SNR (dB → linear) → Shannon; (c) assume all subchannels on → solve γ₀ → check every γj ≥ γ₀ → if not, drop the worst one and recompute.
Key step
(c) The first γ₀ = 0.656 > 0.5, so subchannel 2 is dropped; with one subchannel left, P₁ = P.
Common mistakes
Putting the SNR in dB into log₂; forgetting that doubling the bandwidth doubles the noise; not checking γj ≥ γ₀ and using a negative power.
Intuition
With fixed power, doubling the bandwidth halves the SNR, so capacity grows by less than a factor of two. Water-filling gives up on very poor subchannels and concentrates the power on the good ones.
Link
Practice 5 Q1 (water-filling over three subchannels); Practice 2 Q3 (Shannon capacity and SINR); Practice 7 Q1 (d).
模拟 C · Q1

宏分集：中断概率降多少、相当于省了多少 dB

Original question · 中英对照Mock Quiz C (self-written)

A mobile station is served by two base stations at the same distance. From each base station the average received power due to path loss alone is −95 dBm, and the receiver needs at least −105 dBm to avoid outage. Each link experiences log-normal shadowing with a standard deviation of 8 dB, and the shadowing of the two links is statistically independent.

一个移动台由两个距离相同的基站服务。只考虑路径损耗时，从每个基站收到的平均功率都是 −95 dBm，接收机至少需要 −105 dBm 才不中断。每条链路都有标准差为 8 dB 的对数正态阴影，两条链路的阴影统计独立。

(a) Find the outage probability if the mobile can use only one base station. (3 marks)

(a) 如果移动台只能用一个基站，求中断概率。（3 分）

(b) With macro-diversity, outage occurs only when both received powers fall below −105 dBm. Find the outage probability. (3 marks)

(b) 采用宏分集时，只有两个接收功率都低于 −105 dBm 才算中断。求中断概率。（3 分）

(c) How large would the margin of a single base station have to be to give the same outage probability as in (b)? Hence state the gain of macro-diversity in dB. (4 marks)

(c) 单个基站的余量要多大，才能达到和 (b) 一样的中断概率？据此说明宏分集带来多少 dB 的增益。（4 分）

P_out = Q(余量/σ)（必背）独立 → 相乘Q 函数反查讲义第 2 章 p.12–16；第 4 章 p.3–4
题意讲解：这道题到底要干嘛

场景题目在讲一件什么事

手机站在两个基站中间，离两边一样远，能同时用两个基站（宏分集）。每条链路都有阴影，而且两边的阴影互不相关。

题目先问只用一个基站时断线有多频繁，再问两个一起用时降到多少，最后问：要让单个基站也这么可靠，得多给多少 dB 的余量。

符号题目里每个量是什么

−95 dBm只考虑路径损耗时，每个基站送来的平均功率
−105 dBm门限：低于它就中断
σ = 8 dB阴影的标准差
统计独立一边被挡和另一边被挡没有关系，所以概率可以相乘

要求每一问在问什么

  • (a) 单个基站：余量 = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB，中断 = Q(余量/σ)。
  • (b) 宏分集：两个都低于门限才中断，独立就把两个概率相乘。
  • (c) 反过来：(b) 的中断概率，如果只靠一个基站来达到，需要多大的余量？和实际的 10 dB 比，差值就是宏分集的"增益"。

注意读题容易误会的地方

  • 中断是"两个都低于门限"，不是"任意一个"。
  • (c) 反查 Q 表用的是 (b) 的概率。

目的这道题想让你明白什么

宏分集让中断概率变成乘积，等价于白得了几 dB 的余量；前提是阴影独立。

一句话：两个基站、阴影独立：(a) 单基站 Q(10/8)，(b) 两个相乘，(c) 反查 Q 表求单基站需要的余量，差值就是宏分集增益。

思路：阴影 → 单个基站的中断是 Q(余量/σ)；两条链路独立，都低于门限才中断 → 相乘；(c) 反过来，用 (b) 的概率反查 Q 表，看单个基站要多大余量才能做到。

  1. (a) 余量 = 平均功率 − 门限 = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB。
    P_out,1 = Q(10 / 8) = Q(1.25) = 0.106
  2. (b) 两条链路阴影独立，两个都低于门限的概率 = 各自概率相乘：
    P_out,2 = [Q(1.25)]² = 0.1056² = 1.12 × 10⁻²
  3. (c) 单个基站要达到 Q(x) = 0.0112：查 Q 表，Q(2.28) = 0.0113、Q(2.29) = 0.0110，取 x ≈ 2.29。所需余量 = 2.29 × 8 ≈ 18.3 dB。
  4. 实际余量只有 10 dB，宏分集相当于多给了 18.3 − 10 ≈ 8.3 dB 的余量，这就是宏分集增益。
(a) ≈ 0.106（10.6%）；(b) ≈ 1.12 × 10⁻²（1.1%）；(c) 单基站要约 18.3 dB 余量，宏分集增益约 8.3 dB
官方答案：无（自编模拟题）。所有数字已用 src/p2build/verify_mock.py 验算。
中断从 10.6% 降到 1.1%，相当于不加发射功率就白得了 8 dB 多的余量。前提是两条链路的阴影独立；有共同阴影时（P2 Q2 (c)）效果会打折扣。
知识讲解 + 详细解析：阴影中断、宏分集与等效 dB 增益

知识讲解阴影中断为什么是 Q(余量/σ)

阴影（shadowing，大尺度衰落 large-scale fading）是楼房、树木等障碍物挡住信号，使接收功率在平均值附近随机起伏。讲义第 2 章 p.11–12：阴影 ψdB 服从均值 0、标准差 σ 的高斯分布（所以叫对数正态 log-normal），接收功率 Pr,dB = 不含阴影的平均功率 + ψdB。

中断（outage，p.14–15）= 接收功率低于门限 Pmin。把它整理成"随机变量 < −x"的形式：P(ψdB < −x)，其中 x = 平均功率 − 门限，叫余量（margin）。高斯分布关于 0 对称，P(ψ < −x) = P(ψ > x) = Q(x/σ)。老师要求掌握这个整理技巧，第 3、6、7 章算误码率都用它；Pout = Q(x/σ) 要背。

Q 函数（Q-function）：Q(k) = 标准高斯随机变量大于 k 的概率，k 越大 Q 越小。查表时行看前两位（1.2），列看第三位（0.05），Q(1.25) = 0.1056。老师说 Q 表一定会考，正着查、倒着查都要会。

知识讲解宏分集，以及"独立就相乘"

分集（diversity，第 4 章 p.2–3）：同一信号经过几条不同的信道送到，几条同时都很差的可能性很小。讲义 p.3：M 个独立分支时 Pout,diversity = PoutM。宏分集（macroscopic diversity，p.4）用的是几个不同位置的基站，对付的是阴影：一个方向被大楼挡住时，另一个方向的基站不一定被挡。

为什么能相乘：第 1b 章 p.6，A、B 独立时 P(A ∩ B) = P(A)P(B)。"两条链路都低于门限"就是两个独立事件同时发生。

老师课上（Practice 4 第 1 题）说宏分集的解释题答两点就够：① 宏分集是什么（同一信息经不同基站 / 路径传送）；② 为什么能降低中断（所有路径同时中断的可能性很小）。

详细解析(a) 单个基站

余量 x = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB（dBm − dBm = dB）
x/σ = 10/8 = 1.25
Pout,1 = Q(1.25) = 0.1056 ≈ 10.6%

为什么除以 σ 而不是 σ²：Q 函数的参数是"离均值几个标准差"，标准差 σ = 8 dB 和余量单位相同；σ² = 64 dB² 单位都对不上。

详细解析(b) 两个基站：独立，相乘

Pout,2 = P(链路 1 中断) × P(链路 2 中断) = 0.1056 × 0.1056 ≈ 0.0112 = 1.12 × 10⁻²

要写出依据："两条链路的阴影独立，所以两者同时低于门限的概率等于各自概率的乘积"。老师说只写数字不说理由只给一半分。

详细解析(c) 反查 Q 表：把"概率"换回"dB"

  1. 做什么：找单个基站需要的余量 m，使 Q(m/8) = 0.0112。为什么：(b) 的可靠性如果只靠一个基站得到，只能加大余量（加发射功率或缩短距离）；两种做法一比，就知道宏分集"值"多少 dB。
  2. 反查 Q 表：Q(2.28) = 0.0113，Q(2.29) = 0.0110，0.0112 夹在两者之间。线性插值得 x ≈ 2.28 + 0.01 × (0.0113 − 0.0112)/(0.0113 − 0.0110) ≈ 2.283；用不舍入的概率精确算是 2.2848。本题解答取 2.29，取 2.28 或 2.29 都可以，结果都约 18.3 dB。
  3. 换回 dB：
    所需余量 = x σ ≈ 2.285 × 8 = 18.3 dB（取 2.28：18.24 dB；取 2.29：18.32 dB）
    宏分集增益 = 18.3 − 10 = 8.3 dB

含义：一点发射功率都不加，靠两个基站就相当于余量从 10 dB 提高到约 18.3 dB。

检查合不合理、还能怎么问

  • (b) 的概率比 (a) 小 → 需要的余量比 10 dB 大 → 增益为正，方向对。
  • 极端情况：两条链路完全相关（总是一起被挡），Pout 还是 0.106，增益 0 dB；完全独立是最理想的情况。P2 Q2 (c) 是部分相关（有共同阴影）的版本。
  • 三个独立基站：Pout = 0.1056³ ≈ 1.18 × 10⁻³，反查 x ≈ 3.04，相当于余量 24.3 dB，增益 14.3 dB。第二个基站带来 8.3 dB，第三个只再加约 6.0 dB，越加越少。

易错常见丢分点

  • 理解成"任意一个低于门限就中断"：1 − (1 − 0.106)² = 0.200，反而比单基站更差，方向就错了。
  • 用方差除：10/64，Q 的参数不对。
  • 反查时用了 (a) 的 0.106，而不是 (b) 的 0.0112。
  • 把 18.3 dB 当成增益：18.3 dB 是单基站所需的余量，增益要减去已有的 10 dB。
  • 没写"独立"这个前提就直接相乘。

考试英文写法

(a) The margin is x = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB. Since ψdB ~ N(0, σ²) with σ = 8 dB, Pout = P(ψdB < −x) = Q(x/σ) = Q(1.25) = 0.106.

(b) The shadowing on the two links is independent, so the probability that both received powers are below −105 dBm is the product: Pout = Q(1.25)² = 0.106² ≈ 1.12 × 10⁻².

(c) A single base station needs Q(m/σ) = 0.0112. From the Q table, m/σ ≈ 2.29, so m ≈ 2.29 × 8 ≈ 18.3 dB. The actual margin is 10 dB, so the macro-diversity gain is about 18.3 − 10 ≈ 8.3 dB: two independent base stations give the same reliability as about 8.3 dB of extra margin, without extra transmit power.

一句话：单基站 Q(10/8) = 0.106；两个独立基站相乘得 0.0112；反查 Q 表，单基站要约 18.3 dB 余量才能做到，所以宏分集等效增益约 8.3 dB。
◆ 本题知识点
考什么
阴影中断 Pout = Q(余量/σ)（必背）、独立事件相乘、Q 函数反查；宏分集为什么有用（第 2 章 p.12–16；第 4 章 p.3–4）。
前置知识
余量 = 平均功率 − 门限（dBm − dBm = dB）；独立：P(A 且 B) = P(A)P(B)；Q 表正查、反查。
解题套路
(a) 余量 ÷ σ → 查 Q；(b) 平方；(c) 用 (b) 的概率反查 x → 所需余量 = xσ → 和实际余量相减得到宏分集增益。
关键一步
Q(1.25) = 0.1056 → 平方 0.0112 → 反查 x ≈ 2.29 → 18.3 dB，比实际的 10 dB 多出约 8.3 dB。
易错
中断要求两个都低于门限，不是任意一个；Q 的参数是余量除以标准差 σ，不是方差；反查时用的是 (b) 的概率，不是 (a) 的。
直觉
宏分集的好处来自"一个坏了还有另一个"：两条链路同时被挡住的概率是各自概率的乘积。阴影相关时，乘积不成立，效果打折扣（P2 Q2 (c)）。
联系
P2 Q2 (b) 的数字版；P4 Q1 用文字解释同一件事；模拟 A · Q2 是单基站的阴影中断。

Approach: Shadowing → a single base station's outage is Q(margin/σ); the two links are independent and outage needs both below the threshold → multiply; (c) works backwards from the probability in (b) with the Q table.

  1. (a) Margin = average power − threshold = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB.
    Pout,1 = Q(10 / 8) = Q(1.25) = 0.106
  2. (b) Independent shadowing, so the probability that both links are below the threshold is the product:
    Pout,2 = [Q(1.25)]² = 0.1056² = 1.12 × 10⁻²
  3. (c) A single base station needs Q(x) = 0.0112: the Q table gives Q(2.28) = 0.0113 and Q(2.29) = 0.0110, so x ≈ 2.29. Required margin = 2.29 × 8 ≈ 18.3 dB.
  4. The actual margin is only 10 dB, so macro-diversity is worth about 18.3 − 10 ≈ 8.3 dB of extra margin: the macro-diversity gain.
(a) ≈ 0.106 (10.6%); (b) ≈ 1.12 × 10⁻² (1.1%); (c) a single base station would need about 18.3 dB of margin; macro-diversity gain ≈ 8.3 dB
Official answer: none (self-written mock question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
Outage drops from 10.6% to 1.1%, as if more than 8 dB of margin were gained without extra transmit power. This assumes independent shadowing; with common shadowing (P2 Q2 (c)) the gain is smaller.
Concept + step by step: shadowing outage, macro-diversity, gain in dB

ConceptWhy shadowing outage is Q(margin/σ)

Shadowing (large-scale fading) is caused by obstacles such as buildings and trees blocking the signal, so the received power fluctuates randomly around its average. Ch 2 p.11–12: the shadowing ψdB is Gaussian with mean 0 and standard deviation σ (hence log-normal), and the received power is Pr,dB = average power without shadowing + ψdB.

Outage (p.14–15) = received power below the threshold Pmin. Rearrange it into the form "random variable < −x": P(ψdB < −x), where x = average power − threshold is the margin. The Gaussian is symmetric about 0, so P(ψ < −x) = P(ψ > x) = Q(x/σ). The lecturer wants you to master this rearrangement, since Ch 3, 6 and 7 use it for every error probability; memorise Pout = Q(x/σ).

The Q-function: Q(k) = the probability that a standard Gaussian random variable exceeds k; the larger k, the smaller Q. In the table, the row gives the first two digits (1.2) and the column the third (0.05): Q(1.25) = 0.1056. The lecturer said the Q table will definitely be tested, both forward and inverse lookup.

ConceptMacro-diversity, and "independent means multiply"

Diversity (Ch 4 p.2–3): the same signal arrives over several different channels, and it is unlikely that all of them are bad at the same time. Slide p.3: with M independent branches, Pout,diversity = PoutM. Macroscopic diversity (p.4) uses several base stations at different locations and fights shadowing: when one direction is blocked by a building, the base station in another direction is not necessarily blocked.

Why we may multiply: Ch 1b p.6, if A and B are independent then P(A ∩ B) = P(A)P(B). "Both links below the threshold" is two independent events happening together.

The lecturer said in class (Practice 4 Q1) that a macro-diversity explanation needs only two points: ① what macro-diversity is (the same information sent via different base stations / paths); ② why it reduces outage (it is very unlikely that all paths are in outage at the same time).

Step by step(a) One base station

margin x = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB (dBm − dBm = dB)
x/σ = 10/8 = 1.25
Pout,1 = Q(1.25) = 0.1056 ≈ 10.6%

Why divide by σ and not σ²: the argument of Q is "how many standard deviations from the mean"; σ = 8 dB has the same unit as the margin, whereas σ² = 64 dB² does not even match in unit.

Step by step(b) Two base stations: independent, so multiply

Pout,2 = P(link 1 in outage) × P(link 2 in outage) = 0.1056 × 0.1056 ≈ 0.0112 = 1.12 × 10⁻²

Write the reason: "the shadowing on the two links is independent, so the probability that both are below the threshold is the product of the individual probabilities". The lecturer said a number without a reason gets only half the marks.

Step by step(c) Inverse Q lookup: turn the probability back into dB

  1. What: find the margin m a single base station needs so that Q(m/8) = 0.0112. Why: if the reliability of (b) had to come from one base station, the only way is a larger margin (more transmit power or a shorter distance); comparing the two approaches tells us how many dB macro-diversity is "worth".
  2. Inverse lookup: Q(2.28) = 0.0113 and Q(2.29) = 0.0110, and 0.0112 lies between them. Linear interpolation gives x ≈ 2.28 + 0.01 × (0.0113 − 0.0112)/(0.0113 − 0.0110) ≈ 2.283; with the unrounded probability the exact value is 2.2848. The solution above uses 2.29; either 2.28 or 2.29 is fine, both give about 18.3 dB.
  3. Back to dB:
    required margin = x σ ≈ 2.285 × 8 = 18.3 dB (with 2.28: 18.24 dB; with 2.29: 18.32 dB)
    macro-diversity gain = 18.3 − 10 = 8.3 dB

Meaning: without adding any transmit power, two base stations act like raising the margin from 10 dB to about 18.3 dB.

CheckDoes it make sense, and other ways to ask

  • The probability in (b) is smaller than in (a) → the required margin is larger than 10 dB → the gain is positive: right direction.
  • Extreme case: fully correlated links (always blocked together) still give Pout = 0.106 and a gain of 0 dB; fully independent links are the best case. P2 Q2 (c) is the partly correlated (common shadowing) version.
  • Three independent base stations: Pout = 0.1056³ ≈ 1.18 × 10⁻³, inverse lookup x ≈ 3.04, i.e. a margin of 24.3 dB and a gain of 14.3 dB. The second base station brings 8.3 dB, the third only about 6.0 dB more: diminishing returns.

PitfallsWhere marks are usually lost

  • Reading it as "outage if either link is below the threshold": 1 − (1 − 0.106)² = 0.200, worse than one base station, so the direction is wrong.
  • Dividing by the variance: 10/64 is the wrong Q argument.
  • Using 0.106 from (a) in the inverse lookup instead of 0.0112 from (b).
  • Calling 18.3 dB the gain: 18.3 dB is the margin a single base station needs; the gain subtracts the existing 10 dB.
  • Multiplying without stating the assumption of independence.

ExamHow to write it in English

(a) The margin is x = −95 − (−105) = 10 dB. Since ψdB ~ N(0, σ²) with σ = 8 dB, Pout = P(ψdB < −x) = Q(x/σ) = Q(1.25) = 0.106.

(b) The shadowing on the two links is independent, so the probability that both received powers are below −105 dBm is the product: Pout = Q(1.25)² = 0.106² ≈ 1.12 × 10⁻².

(c) A single base station needs Q(m/σ) = 0.0112. From the Q table, m/σ ≈ 2.29, so m ≈ 2.29 × 8 ≈ 18.3 dB. The actual margin is 10 dB, so the macro-diversity gain is about 18.3 − 10 ≈ 8.3 dB: two independent base stations give the same reliability as about 8.3 dB of extra margin, without extra transmit power.

In one line: one base station gives Q(10/8) = 0.106; two independent base stations multiply to 0.0112; the inverse Q lookup shows one base station would need about 18.3 dB of margin, so the macro-diversity gain is about 8.3 dB.
◆ Key points
What it tests
Shadowing outage Pout = Q(margin/σ) (memorise), multiplying independent events, inverse Q lookup; why macro-diversity helps (Ch 2 p.12–16; Ch 4 p.3–4).
Background
Margin = average power − threshold (dBm − dBm = dB); independent: P(A and B) = P(A)P(B); forward and inverse Q table lookup.
Method
(a) margin ÷ σ → Q; (b) square it; (c) inverse Q of the probability in (b) → required margin = xσ → subtract the actual margin to get the macro-diversity gain.
Key step
Q(1.25) = 0.1056 → squared 0.0112 → inverse x ≈ 2.29 → 18.3 dB, about 8.3 dB more than the actual 10 dB.
Common mistakes
Outage needs both links below the threshold, not either; Q takes margin over the standard deviation σ, not the variance; the inverse lookup uses the probability from (b), not (a).
Intuition
Macro-diversity works because "if one fails, the other is still there": both links blocked at once has probability equal to the product. With correlated shadowing the product no longer holds and the gain shrinks (P2 Q2 (c)).
Link
A numerical version of P2 Q2 (b); P4 Q1 explains the same idea in words; mock A·Q2 is single-base-station shadowing outage.
模拟 C · Q2

衰落类型判断，从观测次数求 LCR 和每秒错误符号数

Original question · 中英对照Mock Quiz C (self-written)

A system transmits 50 k symbols/s (take the signal bandwidth to be about 50 kHz) over a mobile channel with a coherence bandwidth of 200 kHz and a maximum Doppler shift of 100 Hz.

某系统以 50 k 符号/秒发送（信号带宽取约 50 kHz），信道的相干带宽为 200 kHz，最大多普勒频移为 100 Hz。

(a) Explain how the coherence bandwidth, the Doppler spread and the signal bandwidth determine the type of fading, and classify the fading experienced by this system. (4 marks)

(a) 说明相干带宽、多普勒扩展和信号带宽如何决定衰落类型，并判断本系统经历的衰落类型。（4 分）

(b) Over a period of 10 seconds, deep fades are observed 300 times, and the average fade duration is estimated to be 0.5 ms. Assuming that every symbol inside a deep fade is in error, find the level crossing rate, the fraction of time the signal spends in deep fades, and the average number of symbol errors per second. (4 marks)

(b) 10 秒内观测到 300 次深衰落，平均衰落时长估计为 0.5 ms。假设深衰落期间的每个符号都出错，求电平通过率、信号处于深衰落的时间比例，以及平均每秒错误的符号数。（4 分）

(c) For Rayleigh fading the level crossing rate and the average fade duration are \(L_Z=\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho e^{-\rho^2}\) and \(\bar\tau_Z=\dfrac{e^{\rho^2}-1}{\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho}\). Assuming Rayleigh fading, how far (in dB) below the rms power is the deep-fade threshold? (2 marks)

(c) 瑞利衰落的电平通过率和平均衰落时长分别为 \(L_Z=\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho e^{-\rho^2}\) 和 \(\bar\tau_Z=\dfrac{e^{\rho^2}-1}{\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho}\)。假设为瑞利衰落，深衰落门限比 rms 功率低多少 dB？（2 分）

衰落类型两个维度LCR = 次数 / 时间LCR × AFD = 时间比例讲义第 2 章 p.31–43
题意讲解：这道题到底要干嘛

场景题目在讲一件什么事

已知信道的相干带宽、最大多普勒频移和系统的符号速率，先判断衰落类型；再根据实际观测到的深衰落次数和平均时长，算出错误有多频繁；最后假设是瑞利衰落，反推深衰落门限有多低。

这道题的 (a)(b) 和 2025-26 S2 期末卷 Q1(a)(i)(ii) 是同一类问法。

符号题目里每个量是什么

BC = 200 kHz相干带宽：频率相差多远，衰落就不一样了
fm = 100 Hz最大多普勒频移，也就是多普勒扩展 BD；相干时间 TC ≈ 1/fm
Bs ≈ 50 kHz信号带宽（约等于符号速率），Ts = 20 µs
LCR电平通过率：每秒掉进深衰落几次
AFD平均衰落时长：每次在深衰落里待多久
ρ门限幅度 / rms 幅度；ρ² 是功率比

要求每一问在问什么

  • (a) 先讲判断规则，再套到本题：Bs 和 BC 比定平坦还是频率选择性；BD 和 Bs 比（等价于 TC 和 Ts 比）定慢还是快。
  • (b) LCR = 次数 ÷ 观测时间；乘以 AFD 得到在深衰落里的时间比例；AFD ÷ Ts 是每次衰落覆盖几个符号；再乘 LCR 就是每秒错几个。
  • (c) 把题目给的 LCR 和 AFD 两个公式相乘，fm 会消掉，剩下 1 − e−ρ² = 时间比例，解出 ρ²，再换成 dB。

注意读题容易误会的地方

  • 衰落类型两个维度都要答，只答一个只给一半分。
  • LCR 是"每秒"的次数，300 次要除以 10 秒。
  • ρ² 才是功率比，"比 rms 功率低多少 dB"用 10 log₁₀(ρ²)。

目的这道题想让你明白什么

衰落类型看两个维度；LCR 和 AFD 一个管"多频繁"、一个管"多长"，相乘就是信号低于门限的时间比例。

一句话：用 B_C、B_D、B_s 判断平坦慢衰落；由观测次数求 LCR、时间比例和每秒错误数；再用 1 − e^(−ρ²) 反推门限低多少 dB。

思路：(a) 频率维度看信号带宽和相干带宽，时间维度看多普勒扩展和信号带宽，两个都要答；(b) LCR 就是"每秒掉进深衰落几次"，乘以每次持续多久，就是在深衰落里的时间比例；(c) 把题目给的两个公式相乘，fm 消掉，只剩 ρ。

  1. (a) 频率维度：信号带宽 Bs 小于相干带宽 BC → 平坦衰落（整个信号一起起伏）；Bs 大于 BC → 频率选择性衰落（不同频率衰落不同，有 ISI）。时间维度：多普勒扩展 BD（≈ fm）远小于 Bs（等价于相干时间 TC 远大于符号时长 Ts）→ 慢衰落；BD 接近或大于 Bs → 快衰落。
  2. 本题：Bs ≈ 50 kHz < BC = 200 kHz → 平坦；BD = 100 Hz ≪ 50 kHz（TC ≈ 1/fm = 10 ms ≫ Ts = 20 µs）→ 慢。结论：平坦慢衰落。
  3. (b) LCR = 300 次 / 10 s = 30 次/秒。
    深衰落时间比例 = LCR × AFD = 30 × 0.5 ms = 0.015（1.5%）
  4. Ts = 1/50k = 20 µs，每次深衰落覆盖 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 25 个符号。
    每秒错误符号数 = 30 × 25 = 750（也等于 1.5% × 50 000）
  5. (c) 两式相乘：\(L_Z\bar\tau_Z=e^{-\rho^2}(e^{\rho^2}-1)=1-e^{-\rho^2}=0.015\)，所以 \(\rho^2=\ln\frac{1}{0.985}=0.0151\)。ρ² 是门限功率和 rms 功率之比：
    10 log₁₀(0.0151) = −18.2 dB，门限比 rms 功率低约 18.2 dB（ρ = 0.123）
(a) 平坦（B_s < B_C）、慢（B_D ≪ B_s）衰落；(b) LCR = 30 次/s，深衰落占 1.5% 的时间，每秒约 750 个错误符号；(c) 门限比 rms 功率低约 18.2 dB
官方答案：无（自编模拟题）。所有数字已用 src/p2build/verify_mock.py 验算。
自检：把 ρ = 0.123 代回 LCR 公式，fm = 30 / (√(2π) × 0.123 × e−0.0151) ≈ 99 Hz，和题目给的 100 Hz 吻合。错误是成片出现的（每次连续 25 个），所以要靠交织把它们打散。
知识讲解 + 详细解析：衰落类型两个维度、LCR 与 AFD

知识讲解衰落类型：两个维度，各比一次

小尺度衰落要从两个互不相关的方向判断（讲义第 2 章 p.31–39），老师说"问衰落类型只答一个维度只给一半分"：

维度比较什么平坦 / 慢频率选择性 / 快讲义
频率维（由时延扩展引起，时间色散）信号带宽 Bs 和相干带宽 BC（等价：στ 和 Ts）Bs < BC → 平坦衰落（flat）Bs > BC → 频率选择性衰落（frequency-selective），有 ISIp.31–33
时间维（由多普勒扩展引起，频率色散）多普勒扩展 BD 和 Bs（等价：相干时间 TC 和 Ts）BD ≪ Bs（Ts ≪ TC）→ 慢衰落（slow）BD 接近或大于 Bs → 快衰落（fast）p.35–37

符号含义：相干带宽（coherence bandwidth）BC 是"两个频率相差多远，衰落就不再相关"（p.31）；多普勒扩展（Doppler spread）BD = fm，fm = v fc/c 是最大多普勒频移（p.34–35）；相干时间（coherence time）TC ≈ 1/fm，是信道"基本不变"的时长（p.36）。

为什么这样判断：Bs < BC 时，整个信号带宽里的衰落几乎一样，信道只是把整个信号乘上一个复数，不变形；Ts ≪ TC 时，一个符号期间信道几乎不变，幅度和相位可以看成常数（p.37）。

详细解析(a) 套到本题

频率维：Bs ≈ 50 kHz < BC = 200 kHz（BC/Bs = 4）→ 平坦
时间维：BD = fm = 100 Hz，Bs/BD = 50 kHz / 100 Hz = 500 → BD ≪ Bs → 慢
等价写法：TC ≈ 1/fm = 1/100 = 10 ms，Ts = 1/50000 = 20 µs，TC/Ts = 500

结论：平坦慢衰落（flat, slow fading）。两个维度各写一句比较和结论，不要把 BD 和 BC 拿来比（那是两个维度混在一起了）。

知识讲解LCR 和 AFD 按定义算（不需要瑞利）

电平通过率（level crossing rate，LCR，p.40）：信号强度每秒向下穿过门限 Zmin 的平均次数，也就是"每秒掉进深衰落几次"。平均衰落时长（average fade duration，AFD，p.42）：每次掉下去以后在门限下面待的平均时间 = 各次衰落时长之和 ÷ 衰落次数。

p.43 把两者联系起来：AFD = P(z < Zmin) / LZ，即 LZ × AFD = 信号低于门限的时间比例。直观理解：每秒掉下去 30 次，每次 0.5 ms，每秒就有 15 ms 在深衰落里。

老师课上强调（今年 Quiz 第 2 题的常见错误）：题目没说是瑞利衰落时，LCR、AFD 要按定义算（他比作"算平均价格"）；含 fm、ρ 的那两个公式只适用于瑞利衰落。本题 (b) 就是按定义算，(c) 才假设瑞利。

详细解析(b) 每一步做什么、为什么

  1. LCR：次数除以观测时间，因为 LCR 的单位是"次/秒"。
    LCR = 300 次 ÷ 10 s = 30 次/s
  2. 时间比例：LCR × AFD（每秒几次 × 每次多久 = 每秒有多少时间在深衰落里）。AFD 先换成秒。
    时间比例 = 30 × 0.5 × 10⁻³ = 0.015 = 1.5%
  3. 每次衰落错几个符号：AFD 除以符号时长（老师说给的是符号速率时就乘 Rs）。
    Ts = 1/Rs = 1/50000 = 20 µs
    每次衰落的符号数 = 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 0.5 × 10⁻³ × 50000 = 25
  4. 每秒错误符号数：每秒衰落次数 × 每次错的个数。
    每秒错误符号数 = 30 × 25 = 750
    另一条路：1.5% × 50000 = 750（一致）
    符号错误率 SER = 750 / 50000 = 1.5 × 10⁻²

讲义 p.45–47 的例题是同一个套路（AFD × Rs 得每次错的个数，再乘 LCR）。

详细解析(c) 两个瑞利公式相乘，fm 消掉

ρ = Zmin/Zrms 是门限幅度和 rms 幅度之比（老师说解释 ρ 时一定要提到 Zmin 和 Zrms），ρ² 才是功率比。做法：\(L_Z\bar\tau_Z=\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho e^{-\rho^2}\cdot\dfrac{e^{\rho^2}-1}{\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho}=1-e^{-\rho^2}\)。为什么可以这样：由 p.43，LZ τ̄Z 就是 P(z < Zmin)，而瑞利衰落下这个概率是 1 − e−ρ²（就是瑞利中断公式 1 − e−γ₀/γ̄，ρ² = γ₀/γ̄）。

1 − e−ρ² = 0.015 → e−ρ² = 0.985 → ρ² = ln(1/0.985) = 0.01511
门限比 rms 功率：10 log₁₀(0.01511) = −18.2 dB
ρ = √0.01511 = 0.123（幅度比；20 log₁₀0.123 = −18.2 dB，同一个答案）

计算器提示：ln 是自然对数键（ln），不是 log。ρ² 很小时 1 − e−ρ² ≈ ρ²，所以 ρ² ≈ 0.015，10 log₁₀0.015 = −18.24 dB，可以用来快速估算。

检验：把 ρ 代回 LCR 公式反推 fm = 30 / (√(2π) × 0.123 × e−0.0151) ≈ 98.8 Hz，和题目给的 100 Hz 吻合，说明这组数据确实像瑞利衰落。

易错常见丢分点

  • 衰落类型只答"平坦"或只答"慢"：只给一半分。
  • LCR 写成 300：忘了除以 10 秒。
  • AFD 没换成秒：30 × 0.5 = 15，"时间比例"大于 1，明显不对。
  • (b) 去套瑞利公式：题目给了观测数据，按定义算就行。
  • (c) 把 ρ 当功率比：10 log₁₀0.123 = −9.1 dB 是错的；功率比用 ρ²，或者用 20 log₁₀ρ。

考试英文写法

(a) Frequency dimension: the signal bandwidth Bs ≈ 50 kHz is smaller than the coherence bandwidth BC = 200 kHz, so all frequency components fade together: flat fading. Time dimension: the Doppler spread BD = fm = 100 Hz ≪ Bs; equivalently the coherence time TC ≈ 1/fm = 10 ms ≫ Ts = 20 µs, so the channel is constant over a symbol: slow fading. The system experiences flat, slow fading.

(b) LCR = 300/10 = 30 fades per second. Fraction of time in deep fades = LCR × AFD = 30 × 0.5 ms = 0.015 (1.5%). Each fade covers AFD/Ts = 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 25 symbols, so there are 30 × 25 = 750 symbol errors per second.

(c) Multiplying the two formulas, LZ τ̄Z = 1 − e−ρ² = 0.015, so ρ² = ln(1/0.985) = 0.0151, where ρ = Zmin/Zrms. In dB, 10 log₁₀(0.0151) = −18.2 dB: the threshold is about 18.2 dB below the rms power.

一句话：衰落类型两个维度各比一次（Bs 对 BC，BD 对 Bs）；LCR = 次数 ÷ 时间，LCR × AFD = 时间比例；瑞利下 1 − e−ρ² = 时间比例，解出 ρ² 再取 10 log₁₀。
◆ 本题知识点
考什么
用 BC、BD、Bs 判断衰落类型（两个维度）；LCR 和 AFD 的含义与换算；瑞利下 LCR × AFD = 1 − e−ρ²（第 2 章 p.31–43）。2025-26 S2 Q1(a)(i)(ii) 就是这种题。
前置知识
Bs < BC → 平坦；BD ≪ Bs（TC ≫ Ts）→ 慢；LCR = 每秒往下穿过门限的次数；AFD = 每次在门限下停留的平均时长；ρ 是幅度比，ρ² 是功率比。
解题套路
(a) 两个维度分别比较、分别下结论；(b) LCR = 次数 / 观测时间 → 时间比例 = LCR × AFD → 每次的符号数 = AFD / Ts → 每秒错误数 = LCR × 每次的符号数；(c) 两个公式相乘消掉 fm，解 ρ²。
关键公式
LZ τ̄Z = 1 − e−ρ²；门限比 rms 低 10 log₁₀(1/ρ²) dB。
易错
衰落类型只答一个维度只给一半分（老师说过）；LCR 是"次/秒"，别忘了除以观测时间；ρ² 才是功率比，换 dB 用 10 log₁₀(ρ²)（等价于 20 log₁₀ρ）。
检验
把 ρ 代回 LCR 公式反推 fm ≈ 99 Hz，和题目的 100 Hz 吻合。
联系
Practice 2 第 1 题（AFD）；模拟 A · Q3（时延谱 + 多普勒 + AFD）；讲解页 2.4–2.6 节。

Approach: (a) The frequency dimension compares signal bandwidth with coherence bandwidth, the time dimension compares Doppler spread with signal bandwidth; answer both. (b) LCR is "deep fades per second"; times the duration of each gives the fraction of time in deep fades. (c) Multiply the two given formulas: fm cancels and only ρ remains.

  1. (a) Frequency: signal bandwidth Bs below the coherence bandwidth BC → flat fading (the whole signal fades together); Bs above BC → frequency-selective fading (different frequencies fade differently, ISI). Time: Doppler spread BD (≈ fm) much smaller than Bs (equivalently coherence time TC much longer than the symbol duration Ts) → slow fading; BD close to or above Bs → fast fading.
  2. Here Bs ≈ 50 kHz < BC = 200 kHz → flat; BD = 100 Hz ≪ 50 kHz (TC ≈ 1/fm = 10 ms ≫ Ts = 20 µs) → slow. Conclusion: flat, slow fading.
  3. (b) LCR = 300 fades / 10 s = 30 per second.
    fraction of time in deep fades = LCR × AFD = 30 × 0.5 ms = 0.015 (1.5%)
  4. Ts = 1/50k = 20 µs, so each deep fade covers 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 25 symbols.
    symbol errors per second = 30 × 25 = 750 (also 1.5% × 50 000)
  5. (c) Multiply: \(L_Z\bar\tau_Z=e^{-\rho^2}(e^{\rho^2}-1)=1-e^{-\rho^2}=0.015\), so \(\rho^2=\ln\frac{1}{0.985}=0.0151\). ρ² is the ratio of threshold power to rms power:
    10 log₁₀(0.0151) = −18.2 dB: the threshold is about 18.2 dB below the rms power (ρ = 0.123)
(a) flat (Bs < BC), slow (BD ≪ Bs) fading; (b) LCR = 30 per second, 1.5% of the time in deep fades, about 750 symbol errors per second; (c) the threshold is about 18.2 dB below the rms power
Official answer: none (self-written mock question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
Check: putting ρ = 0.123 back into the LCR formula gives fm = 30 / (√(2π) × 0.123 × e−0.0151) ≈ 99 Hz, matching the 100 Hz given. Errors come in bursts (25 in a row), which is why interleaving is used to spread them out.
Concept + step by step: the two fading dimensions, LCR and AFD

ConceptFading type: two dimensions, one comparison each

Small-scale fading is classified along two independent directions (Ch 2 p.31–39). The lecturer said that answering only one dimension when asked for the fading type gets only half the marks:

DimensionWhat to compareFlat / slowFrequency-selective / fastSlides
Frequency (caused by delay spread, time dispersion)signal bandwidth Bs vs coherence bandwidth BC (equivalently στ vs Ts)Bs < BC → flat fadingBs > BC → frequency-selective fading, with ISIp.31–33
Time (caused by Doppler spread, frequency dispersion)Doppler spread BD vs Bs (equivalently coherence time TC vs Ts)BD ≪ Bs (Ts ≪ TC) → slow fadingBD close to or above Bs → fast fadingp.35–37

Symbols: the coherence bandwidth BC is "how far apart two frequencies must be before their fading is no longer correlated" (p.31); the Doppler spread BD = fm, where fm = v fc/c is the maximum Doppler shift (p.34–35); the coherence time TC ≈ 1/fm is how long the channel stays essentially unchanged (p.36).

Why these rules: when Bs < BC, the fading is almost the same across the whole signal bandwidth, so the channel just multiplies the whole signal by one complex number without distorting it; when Ts ≪ TC, the channel hardly changes during a symbol, so amplitude and phase can be treated as constant (p.37).

Step by step(a) Applying it here

Frequency: Bs ≈ 50 kHz < BC = 200 kHz (BC/Bs = 4) → flat
Time: BD = fm = 100 Hz, Bs/BD = 50 kHz / 100 Hz = 500 → BD ≪ Bs → slow
Equivalently: TC ≈ 1/fm = 1/100 = 10 ms, Ts = 1/50000 = 20 µs, TC/Ts = 500

Conclusion: flat, slow fading. Write one comparison and one conclusion for each dimension; do not compare BD with BC (that mixes the two dimensions).

ConceptLCR and AFD from their definitions (no Rayleigh needed)

The level crossing rate (LCR, p.40) is the average number of times per second the signal strength crosses downwards through the threshold Zmin, i.e. "how many deep fades per second". The average fade duration (AFD, p.42) is the average time spent below the threshold per fade = sum of the fade durations ÷ number of fades.

Slide p.43 links the two: AFD = P(z < Zmin) / LZ, i.e. LZ × AFD = fraction of time below the threshold. Intuition: 30 fades per second, 0.5 ms each, means 15 ms of every second is spent in deep fades.

The lecturer stressed in class (a common mistake in this year's Quiz Q2): if the question does not say Rayleigh fading, compute LCR and AFD from the definitions (he compared it to "computing an average price"); the two formulas with fm and ρ apply only to Rayleigh fading. Part (b) here uses the definitions; only (c) assumes Rayleigh.

Step by step(b) What each step does and why

  1. LCR: count divided by observation time, because LCR is in "per second".
    LCR = 300 fades ÷ 10 s = 30 per second
  2. Fraction of time: LCR × AFD (fades per second × duration of each = time per second spent in deep fades). Convert the AFD to seconds first.
    fraction of time = 30 × 0.5 × 10⁻³ = 0.015 = 1.5%
  3. Symbols lost per fade: AFD divided by the symbol duration (the lecturer said: when the symbol rate is given, multiply by Rs).
    Ts = 1/Rs = 1/50000 = 20 µs
    symbols per fade = 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 0.5 × 10⁻³ × 50000 = 25
  4. Symbol errors per second: fades per second × errors per fade.
    symbol errors per second = 30 × 25 = 750
    Other route: 1.5% × 50000 = 750 (same)
    symbol error rate SER = 750 / 50000 = 1.5 × 10⁻²

The example on slides p.45–47 follows the same recipe (AFD × Rs gives the errors per fade, then multiply by the LCR).

Step by step(c) Multiply the two Rayleigh formulas: fm cancels

ρ = Zmin/Zrms is the ratio of the threshold amplitude to the rms amplitude (the lecturer said that when explaining ρ you must mention Zmin and Zrms); ρ² is the power ratio. Method: \(L_Z\bar\tau_Z=\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho e^{-\rho^2}\cdot\dfrac{e^{\rho^2}-1}{\sqrt{2\pi}f_m\rho}=1-e^{-\rho^2}\). Why this works: by p.43, LZ τ̄Z is P(z < Zmin), and for Rayleigh fading this probability is 1 − e−ρ² (the Rayleigh outage formula 1 − e−γ₀/γ̄ with ρ² = γ₀/γ̄).

1 − e−ρ² = 0.015 → e−ρ² = 0.985 → ρ² = ln(1/0.985) = 0.01511
threshold relative to rms power: 10 log₁₀(0.01511) = −18.2 dB
ρ = √0.01511 = 0.123 (amplitude ratio; 20 log₁₀0.123 = −18.2 dB, the same answer)

Calculator hint: use the natural-log key (ln), not log. For small ρ², 1 − e−ρ² ≈ ρ², so ρ² ≈ 0.015 and 10 log₁₀0.015 = −18.24 dB, a quick estimate.

Check: putting ρ back into the LCR formula gives fm = 30 / (√(2π) × 0.123 × e−0.0151) ≈ 98.8 Hz, matching the 100 Hz given, so the data do look like Rayleigh fading.

PitfallsWhere marks are usually lost

  • Answering only "flat" or only "slow": half marks.
  • LCR written as 300: forgot to divide by 10 seconds.
  • AFD not converted to seconds: 30 × 0.5 = 15, a "fraction of time" above 1, clearly wrong.
  • Using the Rayleigh formulas in (b): the question gives observed data, so the definitions are enough.
  • Treating ρ as a power ratio in (c): 10 log₁₀0.123 = −9.1 dB is wrong; for power use ρ², or 20 log₁₀ρ.

ExamHow to write it in English

(a) Frequency dimension: the signal bandwidth Bs ≈ 50 kHz is smaller than the coherence bandwidth BC = 200 kHz, so all frequency components fade together: flat fading. Time dimension: the Doppler spread BD = fm = 100 Hz ≪ Bs; equivalently the coherence time TC ≈ 1/fm = 10 ms ≫ Ts = 20 µs, so the channel is constant over a symbol: slow fading. The system experiences flat, slow fading.

(b) LCR = 300/10 = 30 fades per second. Fraction of time in deep fades = LCR × AFD = 30 × 0.5 ms = 0.015 (1.5%). Each fade covers AFD/Ts = 0.5 ms / 20 µs = 25 symbols, so there are 30 × 25 = 750 symbol errors per second.

(c) Multiplying the two formulas, LZ τ̄Z = 1 − e−ρ² = 0.015, so ρ² = ln(1/0.985) = 0.0151, where ρ = Zmin/Zrms. In dB, 10 log₁₀(0.0151) = −18.2 dB: the threshold is about 18.2 dB below the rms power.

In one line: compare once in each dimension (Bs vs BC, BD vs Bs); LCR = count ÷ time, LCR × AFD = fraction of time; for Rayleigh, 1 − e−ρ² = that fraction, so solve for ρ² and take 10 log₁₀.
◆ Key points
What it tests
Fading type from BC, BD and Bs (two dimensions); meaning and use of LCR and AFD; for Rayleigh, LCR × AFD = 1 − e−ρ² (Ch 2 p.31–43). 2025-26 S2 Q1(a)(i)(ii) is this type of question.
Background
Bs < BC → flat; BD ≪ Bs (TC ≫ Ts) → slow; LCR = downward crossings of the threshold per second; AFD = average time below the threshold per crossing; ρ is an amplitude ratio, ρ² a power ratio.
Method
(a) Compare and conclude in each dimension separately; (b) LCR = count / observation time → fraction = LCR × AFD → symbols per fade = AFD / Ts → errors per second = LCR × symbols per fade; (c) multiply the two formulas to cancel fm and solve for ρ².
Key formulas
LZ τ̄Z = 1 − e−ρ²; the threshold is 10 log₁₀(1/ρ²) dB below the rms power.
Common mistakes
Answering only one dimension of the fading type gets half marks (the lecturer said so); LCR is per second, so divide by the observation time; ρ² is the power ratio, so in dB use 10 log₁₀(ρ²) (= 20 log₁₀ρ).
Check
Putting ρ back into the LCR formula gives fm ≈ 99 Hz, matching the 100 Hz given.
Link
Practice 2 Q1 (AFD); mock A·Q3 (delay profile + Doppler + AFD); learning page sections 2.4–2.6.
模拟 C · Q3

SNR、SIR、SINR，干扰消除，瑞利信道的中断容量

Original question · 中英对照Mock Quiz C (self-written)

A user's received signal power is 2 mW over a bandwidth of 10 MHz. Co-channel interference from a neighbouring cell arrives with a power of 0.5 mW, and the noise power is 0.05 mW. Treat the interference as Gaussian noise.

某用户在 10 MHz 带宽内的接收信号功率为 2 mW。邻区的同频干扰功率为 0.5 mW，噪声功率为 0.05 mW。把干扰当作高斯噪声处理。

(a) Find the SNR, the SIR and the SINR (in dB), and the Shannon capacity of the link. (4 marks)

(a) 求 SNR、SIR 和 SINR（用 dB 表示），以及该链路的香农容量。（4 分）

(b) An interference canceller removes the interference completely. Find the new capacity. (2 marks)

(b) 干扰消除器把干扰完全去掉，求新的容量。（2 分）

(c) In another cell there is no interference, but the link experiences Rayleigh fading with an average SNR of 20 dB. The capacity that can be maintained with outage probability ε is \(C_\varepsilon=B\log_2\!\big[1+\bar\gamma\ln\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\big]\). Find Cε for ε = 1% and compare it with the capacity of an AWGN channel with the same 20 dB SNR. (4 marks)

(c) 另一个小区没有干扰，但链路经历瑞利衰落，平均 SNR 为 20 dB。在中断概率 ε 下能维持的容量为 \(C_\varepsilon=B\log_2\!\big[1+\bar\gamma\ln\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\big]\)。求 ε = 1% 时的 Cε，并和同样 20 dB SNR 的 AWGN 信道容量比较。（4 分）

SNR / SIR / SINR香农容量（必背）中断容量讲义第 2 章 p.48–51
题意讲解：这道题到底要干嘛

场景题目在讲一件什么事

一个用户同时受到邻区干扰和噪声的影响，先比较 SNR、SIR、SINR 三个比值，算出容量；再看把干扰消掉能提升多少。

最后换一个场景：没有干扰但有瑞利衰落，问在允许 1% 中断的前提下，能保证的容量有多大，和没有衰落时差多少。

符号题目里每个量是什么

2 mW想要的信号功率 S
0.5 mW同频干扰功率 I
0.05 mW噪声功率 N
Cε中断容量：允许 ε 的时间中断时，剩下的时间里能保证的速率（公式题目给了）
γ̄ = 20 dB瑞利信道的平均 SNR

要求每一问在问什么

  • (a) 算三个比值（分母分别是 N、I、I + N）并换成 dB；干扰当高斯噪声，香农公式用 SINR。
  • (b) 干扰被完全消掉，分母只剩噪声，用 SNR 重新算容量。
  • (c) 先算公式里的 γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε))（就是允许 1% 中断时的最小 SNR），再代香农；和同样 20 dB 的 AWGN 容量 B log₂(1 + γ̄) 比较。

注意读题容易误会的地方

  • 有干扰时香农公式里用 SINR，不是 SNR。
  • (c) 里的 ln 是自然对数，20 dB 先换成 100。

目的这道题想让你明白什么

干扰比噪声大得多时系统是"干扰受限"的，消掉干扰收益最大；瑞利衰落要为最坏的时刻留余量，能保证的容量远低于平均 SNR 对应的容量。

一句话：比较 SNR、SIR、SINR 并求香农容量，看消掉干扰后容量提升多少，再求瑞利信道 1% 中断时的容量并和 AWGN 比较。

思路：三个比值只差在分母；干扰当高斯噪声时，香农公式里用 SINR；(c) 公式题目给了，\(\bar\gamma\ln\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\) 其实就是瑞利中断公式反解出的最小 SNR。

  1. (a) SNR = 2 / 0.05 = 40（16.0 dB）；SIR = 2 / 0.5 = 4（6.0 dB）；SINR = 2 /(0.5 + 0.05) = 3.64（5.6 dB）。
  2. 干扰当高斯噪声，香农公式用 SINR：
    C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 3.64) = 10 × 2.213 = 22.1 Mbps
  3. (b) 干扰消除后只剩噪声，用 SNR：
    C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 40) = 10 × 5.358 = 53.6 Mbps（约 2.4 倍）
  4. (c) γ̄ = 20 dB = 100，\(\ln\frac{1}{0.99}=0.01005\)，最小 SNR \(\gamma_{\min}=100\times0.01005=1.005\)（约 0 dB）。
    C_ε = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 1.005) = 10 × 1.004 = 10.0 Mbps
  5. 同样 20 dB 的 AWGN 信道：C = 10 MHz × log₂(101) = 66.6 Mbps。瑞利信道要保证 99% 的时间不中断，只能按约 0 dB 的最小 SNR 设计，容量只有 AWGN 的约 1/6.6；γmin 比 γ̄ 低约 20 dB，正好是 1% 中断对应的衰落余量。
(a) SNR 16.0 dB、SIR 6.0 dB、SINR 5.6 dB，C ≈ 22.1 Mbps；(b) C ≈ 53.6 Mbps；(c) Cε ≈ 10.0 Mbps，远低于 AWGN 的 66.6 Mbps
官方答案：无（自编模拟题）。所有数字已用 src/p2build/verify_mock.py 验算。
干扰（0.5 mW）比噪声（0.05 mW）大 10 倍，所以 SINR ≈ SIR，这种系统叫"干扰受限"：再降噪声没什么用，要降干扰。SINR 一定比 SNR、SIR 都小。
知识讲解 + 详细解析：SINR、香农容量与瑞利中断容量

知识讲解SNR、SIR、SINR：只差在分母

三个比值的分子都是有用信号功率 S，分母不同：

名称定义分母里有什么什么时候用
SNR（signal-to-noise ratio）S/N只有噪声没有干扰，或干扰已被消除
SIR（signal-to-interference ratio）S/I只有干扰蜂窝规划，噪声可忽略时
SINR（signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio）S/(I + N)干扰 + 噪声两者都有，把干扰当噪声处理时

SINR 的定义在讲义第 1a 章 p.16（"Interference: Friend or Foe?"）：干扰当噪声处理时是"敌人"，就用 SINR。第 1a 章老师说不考，但这个定义 Practice 2 第 3 题要用。一个好用的关系：1/SINR = 1/SNR + 1/SIR，所以 SINR 一定比 SNR 和 SIR 都小。

知识讲解香农容量

讲义第 2 章 p.48：AWGN 信道的容量 C = B log₂(1 + γ)（bit/s），γ 是接收 SNR（线性值），B 是带宽。意义：传输速率 R < C 时，存在编码能让误码率任意小；R > C 时做不到。老师说这是通信工程师最基本的公式，必须背。干扰被当成高斯噪声时，它和噪声没有区别，γ 就换成 SINR。

计算器提示：log₂x = log₁₀x / 0.301；dB → 线性：10^(dB/10)。B 用 MHz 代入，C 的单位就是 Mbps。

详细解析(a) 三个比值和容量

SNR = 2 / 0.05 = 40 → 10 log₁₀40 = 16.0 dB
SIR = 2 / 0.5 = 4 → 10 log₁₀4 = 6.0 dB
SINR = 2 / (0.5 + 0.05) = 2 / 0.55 = 3.64 → 10 log₁₀3.64 = 5.6 dB
检验：1/40 + 1/4 = 0.025 + 0.25 = 0.275 = 1/3.64
C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 3.64) = 10 × (log₁₀4.64 / 0.301) = 10 × 2.213 = 22.1 Mbps

为什么用 SINR：题目说"把干扰当高斯噪声"，那接收机看到的"噪声"就是 I + N。功率都是 mW，相除后单位消掉，不用换成 W。

详细解析(b) 干扰完全消除

只剩噪声，用 SNR = 40：C = 10 × log₂41 = 10 × 5.358 = 53.6 Mbps
53.6 / 22.1 ≈ 2.4 倍

为什么提升这么大：干扰 0.5 mW 是噪声 0.05 mW 的 10 倍，SINR ≈ SIR，这种系统叫干扰受限（interference-limited）。对比一下：噪声减半，SINR = 2/0.525，C 只到 22.7 Mbps；干扰减半，SINR = 2/0.3，C 升到 29.4 Mbps。要提升容量，就要对付干扰（干扰消除、多用户检测 MUD、扇区化等）。

知识讲解衰落信道：遍历容量和中断容量

衰落信道里 SNR γ 是随机变量（老师强调这一点贯穿第 2–7 章）。讲义给了两种容量：

  • 遍历容量（ergodic capacity，p.49）：对 B log₂(1 + γ) 求平均。由 Jensen 不等式，它不会超过同样平均 SNR 的 AWGN 容量（老师说 Jensen 只要求懂概念）。
  • 中断容量（outage capacity，p.50，适用于慢衰落）：允许 ε 的时间中断，其余时间保证速率 Cε = B log₂(1 + γmin)，γmin 满足 P(γ < γmin) = ε。

瑞利衰落时 P(γ < γmin) = 1 − e−γmin/γ̄（必背的瑞利中断公式）。令它等于 ε，解出 γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε))（p.51），代进香农公式就是题目给的 Cε。p.51 还说：和 AWGN 达到同样容量需要的衰落余量（fade margin）= 10 log₁₀[1/ln(1/(1 − ε))]，ε = 1% 时是 20 dB。

详细解析(c) 一步一步代

  1. dB 换线性：γ̄ = 10^(20/10) = 100。
  2. 求 γmin：ln 是自然对数。
    ln(1/0.99) = 0.01005
    γmin = 100 × 0.01005 = 1.005（约 0.02 dB，也就是约 0 dB）
  3. 代香农：
    Cε = 10 × log₂(1 + 1.005) = 10 × log₂2.005 = 10 × 1.004 = 10.0 Mbps
  4. 和 AWGN 比：
    CAWGN = 10 × log₂(1 + 100) = 10 × 6.658 = 66.6 Mbps
    66.6 / 10.0 ≈ 6.6 倍
    衰落余量 = 20 − 0.02 = 19.98 ≈ 20 dB

为什么差这么多：要保证 99% 的时间都不中断，只能按"最差的 1%"附近的 SNR（约 0 dB）来定速率，比平均 SNR 低约 20 dB。ε 很小时 ln(1/(1 − ε)) ≈ ε，所以 γmin ≈ γ̄ ε：ε = 1% 就是低 20 dB。p.50 还给了"不出错的平均速率" (1 − ε)Cε = 0.99 × 10.04 = 9.94 Mbps。

易错常见丢分点

  • (a) 用 SNR 代香农：题目有干扰且当噪声处理，要用 SINR。
  • 把 dB 值代进 log₂：log₂(1 + 5.6) 是错的，必须用线性值 3.64。
  • (c) 把 ln 按成 log₁₀：log₁₀(1/0.99) = 0.00436，会得到 γmin = 0.436、C = 5.2 Mbps，错。
  • γ̄ = 20 直接代入（没换成 100）。
  • 只给数字不比较：(c) 要说明 Cε 远小于 AWGN 的原因（衰落余量约 20 dB）。

考试英文写法

(a) SNR = S/N = 2/0.05 = 40 (16.0 dB); SIR = S/I = 2/0.5 = 4 (6.0 dB); SINR = S/(I + N) = 2/0.55 = 3.64 (5.6 dB). Treating the interference as Gaussian noise, C = B log₂(1 + SINR) = 10⁷ × log₂(4.64) = 22.1 Mbps.

(b) With the interference removed, C = B log₂(1 + SNR) = 10⁷ × log₂(41) = 53.6 Mbps, about 2.4 times larger, because the link is interference-limited.

(c) γ̄ = 20 dB = 100. For Rayleigh fading, γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)) = 100 × ln(1/0.99) = 1.005, so Cε = 10⁷ × log₂(2.005) = 10.0 Mbps. An AWGN channel with SNR 20 dB gives C = 10⁷ × log₂(101) = 66.6 Mbps. The outage capacity is much lower because, to avoid outage 99% of the time, the rate must be set by an SNR about 20 dB below the average (the fade margin).

一句话：干扰当噪声就用 SINR = S/(I + N) 代香农；去掉干扰改用 SNR；瑞利中断容量先算 γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε))，ε = 1% 时比 γ̄ 低约 20 dB，所以容量远低于 AWGN。
◆ 本题知识点
考什么
SNR、SIR、SINR 的区别；干扰当高斯噪声时的香农容量；瑞利衰落下的中断容量（第 2 章 p.48–51）。2025-26 S2 Q1(a)(iii) 给了同一个 Cε 公式。
前置知识
SNR = S/N，SIR = S/I，SINR = S/(I + N)；C = B log₂(1 + x)，x 用线性值；瑞利中断 1 − e−γ₀/γ̄（必背），反解得 γ₀ = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε))。
解题套路
(a) 三个比值 → 换 dB → 用 SINR 代香农；(b) I = 0，用 SNR；(c) 先算 γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε))，再代香农，和 AWGN 的 B log₂(1 + γ̄) 比。
关键一步
ε = 1%：ln(1/0.99) = 0.01005，γmin ≈ γ̄/100，也就是比平均低 20 dB，等于衰落余量。
易错
有干扰时用 SINR 不用 SNR；功率单位统一；log₂ 里用线性值；(c) 里的 ln 是自然对数。
直觉
干扰比噪声大 10 倍时 SINR ≈ SIR，系统是"干扰受限"的；瑞利衰落要为最坏的 1% 时间留余量，所以能保证的容量远低于平均 SNR 对应的容量。
联系
Practice 2 第 3 题（SINR、MUD）；模拟 B · Q1（瑞利中断与衰落余量）；讲解页 2.7 节的 SNR/SIR/SINR 和衰落余量补充框。

Approach: The three ratios differ only in the denominator; with interference treated as Gaussian noise, Shannon's formula uses the SINR; in (c) the formula is given, and \(\bar\gamma\ln\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\) is just the minimum SNR obtained by inverting the Rayleigh outage formula.

  1. (a) SNR = 2 / 0.05 = 40 (16.0 dB); SIR = 2 / 0.5 = 4 (6.0 dB); SINR = 2 / (0.5 + 0.05) = 3.64 (5.6 dB).
  2. Interference treated as Gaussian noise, so Shannon uses the SINR:
    C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 3.64) = 10 × 2.213 = 22.1 Mbps
  3. (b) After cancellation only noise remains, so use the SNR:
    C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 40) = 10 × 5.358 = 53.6 Mbps (about 2.4 times)
  4. (c) γ̄ = 20 dB = 100 and \(\ln\frac{1}{0.99}=0.01005\), so the minimum SNR is \(\gamma_{\min}=100\times0.01005=1.005\) (about 0 dB).
    Cε = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 1.005) = 10 × 1.004 = 10.0 Mbps
  5. An AWGN channel with the same 20 dB: C = 10 MHz × log₂(101) = 66.6 Mbps. To stay out of outage 99% of the time the Rayleigh link must be designed for a minimum SNR of about 0 dB, so its capacity is only about 1/6.6 of the AWGN value; γmin is about 20 dB below γ̄, exactly the fade margin for 1% outage.
(a) SNR 16.0 dB, SIR 6.0 dB, SINR 5.6 dB, C ≈ 22.1 Mbps; (b) C ≈ 53.6 Mbps; (c) Cε ≈ 10.0 Mbps, far below the 66.6 Mbps of the AWGN channel
Official answer: none (self-written mock question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
The interference (0.5 mW) is 10 times the noise (0.05 mW), so SINR ≈ SIR: the system is interference-limited, and lowering the noise further barely helps; the interference must be reduced. The SINR is always smaller than both the SNR and the SIR.
Concept + step by step: SINR, Shannon capacity, Rayleigh outage capacity

ConceptSNR, SIR, SINR: only the denominator differs

All three ratios have the useful signal power S on top; the denominators differ:

NameDefinitionWhat is in the denominatorWhen to use it
SNR (signal-to-noise ratio)S/Nnoise onlyno interference, or the interference has been cancelled
SIR (signal-to-interference ratio)S/Iinterference onlycellular planning, when noise is negligible
SINR (signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio)S/(I + N)interference + noiseboth present, interference treated as noise

SINR is defined on Ch 1a p.16 ("Interference: Friend or Foe?"): when interference is treated as noise it is a "foe", and SINR is used. The lecturer said Ch 1a is not examined, but Practice 2 Q3 needs this definition. A handy relation: 1/SINR = 1/SNR + 1/SIR, so SINR is always smaller than both SNR and SIR.

ConceptShannon capacity

Ch 2 p.48: the capacity of an AWGN channel is C = B log₂(1 + γ) (bit/s), where γ is the received SNR (linear value) and B the bandwidth. Meaning: if the rate R < C, codes exist that make the error probability arbitrarily small; if R > C, this is impossible. The lecturer said this is the most basic formula of a communication engineer and must be memorised. When interference is treated as Gaussian noise it is no different from noise, so γ becomes the SINR.

Calculator hint: log₂x = log₁₀x / 0.301; dB → linear: 10^(dB/10). With B in MHz, C comes out in Mbps.

Step by step(a) The three ratios and the capacity

SNR = 2 / 0.05 = 40 → 10 log₁₀40 = 16.0 dB
SIR = 2 / 0.5 = 4 → 10 log₁₀4 = 6.0 dB
SINR = 2 / (0.5 + 0.05) = 2 / 0.55 = 3.64 → 10 log₁₀3.64 = 5.6 dB
Check: 1/40 + 1/4 = 0.025 + 0.25 = 0.275 = 1/3.64
C = 10 MHz × log₂(1 + 3.64) = 10 × (log₁₀4.64 / 0.301) = 10 × 2.213 = 22.1 Mbps

Why SINR: the question says "treat the interference as Gaussian noise", so the "noise" the receiver sees is I + N. All powers are in mW and the units cancel in the ratios, so there is no need to convert to W.

Step by step(b) Interference completely removed

Only noise is left, so use SNR = 40: C = 10 × log₂41 = 10 × 5.358 = 53.6 Mbps
53.6 / 22.1 ≈ 2.4 times

Why such a big jump: the interference (0.5 mW) is 10 times the noise (0.05 mW), so SINR ≈ SIR; such a system is interference-limited. Compare: halving the noise gives SINR = 2/0.525 and C only rises to 22.7 Mbps; halving the interference gives SINR = 2/0.3 and C rises to 29.4 Mbps. To raise the capacity, deal with the interference (interference cancellation, multi-user detection MUD, sectoring, etc.).

ConceptFading channels: ergodic capacity and outage capacity

In a fading channel the SNR γ is a random variable (the lecturer stressed that this runs through Ch 2–7). The slides give two capacities:

  • Ergodic capacity (p.49): the average of B log₂(1 + γ). By Jensen's inequality it cannot exceed the AWGN capacity with the same average SNR (the lecturer said only the concept of Jensen is required).
  • Outage capacity (p.50, for slow fading): allow outage a fraction ε of the time and guarantee the rate Cε = B log₂(1 + γmin) the rest of the time, where P(γ < γmin) = ε.

For Rayleigh fading, P(γ < γmin) = 1 − e−γmin/γ̄ (the Rayleigh outage formula, memorise). Setting it equal to ε gives γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)) (p.51); substituting into Shannon gives the Cε in the question. Slide p.51 also says: the fade margin needed to reach the same capacity as AWGN is 10 log₁₀[1/ln(1/(1 − ε))], which is 20 dB for ε = 1%.

Step by step(c) Substituting step by step

  1. dB to linear: γ̄ = 10^(20/10) = 100.
  2. Find γmin; ln is the natural logarithm.
    ln(1/0.99) = 0.01005
    γmin = 100 × 0.01005 = 1.005 (about 0.02 dB, i.e. about 0 dB)
  3. Into Shannon:
    Cε = 10 × log₂(1 + 1.005) = 10 × log₂2.005 = 10 × 1.004 = 10.0 Mbps
  4. Compare with AWGN:
    CAWGN = 10 × log₂(1 + 100) = 10 × 6.658 = 66.6 Mbps
    66.6 / 10.0 ≈ 6.6 times
    fade margin = 20 − 0.02 = 19.98 ≈ 20 dB

Why the gap is so large: to avoid outage 99% of the time, the rate must be set by an SNR near the "worst 1%" (about 0 dB), about 20 dB below the average. For small ε, ln(1/(1 − ε)) ≈ ε, so γmin ≈ γ̄ ε: ε = 1% means 20 dB lower. Slide p.50 also gives the "average rate without errors" (1 − ε)Cε = 0.99 × 10.04 = 9.94 Mbps.

PitfallsWhere marks are usually lost

  • Using SNR in Shannon in (a): there is interference treated as noise, so use SINR.
  • Putting the dB value into log₂: log₂(1 + 5.6) is wrong; use the linear value 3.64.
  • Pressing log₁₀ instead of ln in (c): log₁₀(1/0.99) = 0.00436 would give γmin = 0.436 and C = 5.2 Mbps, wrong.
  • Substituting γ̄ = 20 directly (not converted to 100).
  • Giving numbers without the comparison: (c) should explain why Cε is far below the AWGN value (a fade margin of about 20 dB).

ExamHow to write it in English

(a) SNR = S/N = 2/0.05 = 40 (16.0 dB); SIR = S/I = 2/0.5 = 4 (6.0 dB); SINR = S/(I + N) = 2/0.55 = 3.64 (5.6 dB). Treating the interference as Gaussian noise, C = B log₂(1 + SINR) = 10⁷ × log₂(4.64) = 22.1 Mbps.

(b) With the interference removed, C = B log₂(1 + SNR) = 10⁷ × log₂(41) = 53.6 Mbps, about 2.4 times larger, because the link is interference-limited.

(c) γ̄ = 20 dB = 100. For Rayleigh fading, γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)) = 100 × ln(1/0.99) = 1.005, so Cε = 10⁷ × log₂(2.005) = 10.0 Mbps. An AWGN channel with SNR 20 dB gives C = 10⁷ × log₂(101) = 66.6 Mbps. The outage capacity is much lower because, to avoid outage 99% of the time, the rate must be set by an SNR about 20 dB below the average (the fade margin).

In one line: with interference treated as noise, put SINR = S/(I + N) into Shannon; without interference use SNR; for Rayleigh outage capacity first find γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)), which for ε = 1% is about 20 dB below γ̄, so the capacity is far below AWGN.
◆ Key points
What it tests
SNR, SIR and SINR; Shannon capacity with interference treated as Gaussian noise; outage capacity in Rayleigh fading (Ch 2 p.48–51). 2025-26 S2 Q1(a)(iii) gives the same Cε formula.
Background
SNR = S/N, SIR = S/I, SINR = S/(I + N); C = B log₂(1 + x) with x linear; Rayleigh outage 1 − e−γ₀/γ̄ (memorise), inverted γ₀ = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)).
Method
(a) Three ratios → dB → Shannon with the SINR; (b) I = 0, use the SNR; (c) γmin = γ̄ ln(1/(1 − ε)) first, then Shannon, and compare with the AWGN B log₂(1 + γ̄).
Key step
For ε = 1%, ln(1/0.99) = 0.01005, so γmin ≈ γ̄/100: 20 dB below the average, which is the fade margin.
Common mistakes
With interference use the SINR, not the SNR; keep the power units consistent; use linear values inside log₂; the ln in (c) is the natural log.
Intuition
When interference is 10 times the noise, SINR ≈ SIR and the system is interference-limited. Rayleigh fading forces a margin for the worst 1% of the time, so the capacity that can be guaranteed is far below the one for the average SNR.
Link
Practice 2 Q3 (SINR, MUD); mock B·Q1 (Rayleigh outage and fade margin); the SNR/SIR/SINR and fade-margin boxes in section 2.7 of the learning page.
专项 · Q1

自由空间模型，以及为什么不能拿它代替简化模型

Original question · 中英对照Focused practice (self-written)

A transmitter radiates 1 W at 900 MHz. Both antennas have unity gain (\(G_t=G_r=1\)).

某发射机在 900 MHz 上发射 1 W，收发天线增益都是 1（\(G_t=G_r=1\)）。

(a) Assuming free-space propagation, find the received power in dBm and the path loss in dB at a distance of 1 km. (3 marks)

(a) 假设自由空间传播，求 1 km 处的接收功率（dBm）和路径损耗（dB）。（3 分）

(b) By how many dB does the free-space received power drop when the distance is doubled to 2 km? By how many more dB when the carrier frequency is then doubled to 1.8 GHz? (3 marks)

(b) 距离加倍到 2 km，自由空间接收功率下降多少 dB？再把载频加倍到 1.8 GHz，又下降多少 dB？（3 分）

(c) For an urban macrocell, the simplified path loss model with \(K_{dB}=-31.5\) dB, \(d_0=1\) m and γ = 3.5 is used instead. Find the received power at 1 km and explain why using the free-space model here would be a serious mistake. (4 marks)

(c) 对城区宏蜂窝，改用简化路径损耗模型：\(K_{dB}=-31.5\) dB，\(d_0=1\) m，γ = 3.5。求 1 km 处的接收功率，并说明为什么这里用自由空间模型是严重的错误。（4 分）

自由空间 Friis 公式P ∝ d⁻²、f⁻²简化模型 vs 自由空间讲义第 2 章 p.3–4、p.8
题意讲解：这道题到底要干嘛

场景题目在讲一件什么事

讲义第 2 章 p.4 的自由空间模型：发射机和接收机之间什么都不挡，功率像吹气球一样均匀撒到越来越大的球面上，所以按 d⁻² 减少。

这道题先用它算一次，再换成城区用的简化路径损耗模型算一次，看看两者差多少。老师提醒过，往年 Quiz 有学生把简化模型的题用成了自由空间模型。

符号题目里每个量是什么

1 W发射功率，换成 30 dBm
900 MHz载频，λ = c/f = 0.333 m
\(G_t=G_r=1\)收发天线增益（0 dB）
\(P_r=P_tG_tG_r(\lambda/4\pi d)^2\)自由空间公式（Friis）
γ = 3.5(c) 里城区的路径损耗指数；自由空间相当于 γ = 2

要求每一问在问什么

  • (a) 用自由空间公式算 1 km 处的接收功率和路径损耗。
  • (b) 距离加倍、频率加倍各让功率少多少 dB：看它们在公式里是几次方。
  • (c) 同一个场景换成简化模型（γ = 3.5）再算，和自由空间比较，解释为什么城区不能用自由空间。

注意读题容易误会的地方

  • 题目说 simplified path loss model 时，不要套自由空间公式。
  • λ/4πd 是幅度比，取 dB 用 20 log₁₀。

目的这道题想让你明白什么

自由空间是理想情况，只适合"什么都不挡"的场合；城区要用简化模型，两者在 1 km 处就能差几十 dB。

一句话：用自由空间公式算 1 km 的接收功率，看距离和频率加倍各少几 dB，再和 γ = 3.5 的简化模型比较，说明为什么不能混用。

思路：自由空间公式 \(P_r=P_tG_tG_r\big(\frac{\lambda}{4\pi d}\big)^2\) 取 dB 计算；距离和频率都以平方出现，各加倍一次就各少 6 dB；(c) 用简化模型算一遍，和自由空间比，差几十 dB。

  1. (a) \(\lambda=c/f=3\times10^8/9\times10^8=0.333\) m，\(P_t\) = 1 W = 30 dBm。
    P_r = 30 + 20 log₁₀(0.333 / (4π × 1000)) = 30 + 20 log₁₀(2.65×10⁻⁵) = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm
    路径损耗 = 30 − (−61.5) = 91.5 dB（也可以用 Part A 的 32.44 + 20 log₁₀900 + 20 log₁₀1 = 91.5 dB 核对）。
  2. (b) \(P_r\propto d^{-2}\)：距离加倍，少 20 log₁₀2 = 6.0 dB（−67.5 dBm）。\(P_r\propto\lambda^2\propto f^{-2}\)：频率再加倍，再少 6.0 dB（−73.6 dBm）。
  3. (c) 简化模型：
    P_r = 30 + (−31.5) − 10 × 3.5 × log₁₀1000 = 30 − 31.5 − 105 = −106.5 dBm
  4. 和自由空间的 −61.5 dBm 差了约 45 dB（3 万多倍）。城区有楼房遮挡、地面反射，功率随距离衰减得快得多（γ = 3.5 而不是 2）；用自由空间会严重高估接收功率，覆盖范围、中断概率全都算错。题目说 simplified path loss model，就一定要用它。
(a) P_r ≈ −61.5 dBm，路径损耗 ≈ 91.5 dB；(b) 各少 6 dB（−67.5 dBm，再到 −73.6 dBm）；(c) −106.5 dBm，比自由空间低约 45 dB，用自由空间会严重高估
官方答案：无（自编模拟题）。所有数字已用 src/p2build/verify_mock.py 验算。
一个巧合：900 MHz 时自由空间在 1 m 处的增益 20 log₁₀(λ/4π) = −31.5 dB，正好和 (c) 用的 KdB 一样。所以两种模型在 1 m 处一样，差别全在指数：每 10 倍距离，自由空间少 20 dB，γ = 3.5 时少 35 dB。老师提醒过，往年 Quiz 有学生把简化模型的题用成了自由空间模型。
知识讲解 + 详细解析：自由空间公式与简化路径损耗模型

知识讲解自由空间（Friis）公式：每个符号是什么

讲义第 2 章 p.4：没有任何遮挡、只有一条直射路径（LOS）时，\(P_r=\dfrac{P_tG_tG_r\lambda^2}{(4\pi)^2d^2}\)。Pt、Pr 是发射、接收功率；Gt、Gr 是收发天线增益（本题都是 1）；λ = c/f 是波长；d 是距离。讲义的两句结论：接收功率和 d² 成反比，和 λ² 成正比（也就是和 f² 成反比）。

取 dB 形式最好算：Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + Gt,dB + Gr,dB + 20 log₁₀(λ/(4πd))。为什么是 20：(λ/4πd)² 取 10 log₁₀ 就等于 20 log₁₀(λ/4πd)。Part A 讲义（p.51）给的经验写法是 L(dB) = 32 + 20 log f(MHz) + 20 log d(km)，更精确的常数是 32.44。

课外补充d² 和 λ² 从哪来（讲义没解释原因）

d² 来自功率在半径为 d 的球面上摊开，球面积 4πd²；λ² 来自接收天线的有效面积和 λ² 成正比，频率越高，同样增益的天线"接住"的功率越少。

知识讲解简化路径损耗模型：自由空间是它的特例

讲义 p.8：Pr,dB = Pt,dB + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀)，γ 是路径损耗指数（path loss exponent），K 和 γ 由实测数据拟合。老师说这个模型线性和 dB 两种形式都要背，考试不给；"simplified path loss model"是专有名词，往年 Quiz 有学生把它用成了自由空间模型。

把自由空间写成同样的形式：取 d₀ = 1 m，KdB = 20 log₁₀(λ/4π)，γ = 2。所以两个模型真正的区别是指数：每 10 倍距离，自由空间少 20 dB，γ = 3.5 时少 35 dB。

详细解析(a) 1 km 处的自由空间接收功率

  1. 求波长：
    λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / (9 × 10⁸) = 0.3333 m
  2. 发射功率换 dBm（老师说 W / mW 没换会扣分）：
    Pt = 1 W = 1000 mW → 10 log₁₀1000 = 30 dBm
  3. 代 dB 公式（d 用米，和 λ 单位一致）：
    λ/(4πd) = 0.3333 / (4π × 1000) = 0.3333 / 12566 = 2.65 × 10⁻⁵
    20 log₁₀(2.65 × 10⁻⁵) = 20 × (−4.576) = −91.5 dB
    Pr = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm；路径损耗 = 30 − (−61.5) = 91.5 dB
  4. 核对：32.44 + 20 log₁₀900 + 20 log₁₀1 = 32.44 + 59.08 + 0 = 91.5 dB（这里 f 用 MHz、d 用 km）。

详细解析(b) 距离加倍、频率加倍

Pr ∝ d⁻²：d 加倍 → 少 20 log₁₀2 = 6.02 dB → −61.53 − 6.02 = −67.55 dBm
Pr ∝ λ² ∝ f⁻²：f 再加倍 → 再少 6.02 dB → −67.55 − 6.02 = −73.6 dBm

规律：某个量在公式里是 n 次方，它加倍时功率变化 n × 3 dB（10 log₁₀2 ≈ 3）。自由空间里 d 和 f 都是 −2 次方，所以各是 −6 dB。

说明：上面解答里写的 −67.6 dBm 是舍入差。精确值是 −67.547 dBm，四舍五入到一位小数是 −67.5 dBm（两位小数 −67.55），相差 0.05 dB，不影响结论。

详细解析(c) 简化模型，以及为什么不能用自由空间

Pr = 30 + (−31.5) − 10 × 3.5 × log₁₀1000 = 30 − 31.5 − 105 = −106.5 dBm
和自由空间相差：−61.5 − (−106.5) = 45 dB（功率比约 3.1 万倍）

为什么正好约 45 dB：900 MHz 时自由空间的 KdB = 20 log₁₀(0.3333/4π) = −31.5 dB，和题目的 KdB 几乎一样，所以两个模型在 1 m 处相同；从 1 m 到 1 km 是 3 个"10 倍"，自由空间少 20 × 3 = 60 dB，简化模型少 35 × 3 = 105 dB，差 45 dB。

为什么是严重错误：城区有楼房遮挡、地面和墙面反射，功率随距离衰减得比自由空间快得多。用自由空间会把接收功率高估约 45 dB，覆盖范围、中断概率、容量全都算错。题目写了 simplified path loss model，就必须用它。另外讲义 p.9 的表里城区宏蜂窝的 γ 是 3.7–6.5（γ 的取值表不用背），实际衰减可能比 γ = 3.5 更快，差距更大。

易错常见丢分点

  • 用 10 log₁₀(λ/4πd)：λ/4πd 是幅度比，要用 20 log₁₀（或者先平方再 10 log₁₀）。
  • 1 W 没换成 30 dBm，直接写 Pr = 1 − 91.5。
  • Friis 公式里 d 代 km（λ 是米）：单位不一致。简化模型 d₀ = 1 m 时 d 也要用米：log₁₀1000 = 3，不是 log₁₀1 = 0（老师说今年 Quiz 有人这样丢了计算分）。
  • (b) 把"再加倍频率"理解成功率增加：f 越高 λ 越小，接收功率越小。

考试英文写法

(a) λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / 9 × 10⁸ = 0.333 m and Pt = 1 W = 30 dBm. In free space, Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)², so Pr,dBm = 30 + 20 log₁₀(0.333/(4π × 1000)) = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm, and the path loss is 91.5 dB.

(b) Pr ∝ d⁻², so doubling d reduces Pr by 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB (to −67.55 dBm). Pr ∝ λ² ∝ f⁻², so doubling f reduces it by another 6 dB (to −73.6 dBm).

(c) Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀) = 30 − 31.5 − 35 log₁₀(1000) = −106.5 dBm, about 45 dB below the free-space value. Free space assumes an unobstructed LOS path with γ = 2, whereas in an urban macrocell the power decays much faster (γ = 3.5) because of obstruction and reflections. Using free space would overestimate the received power by about 45 dB and give completely wrong coverage and outage results.

一句话：自由空间 Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)²，用 20 log₁₀ 算，d、f 加倍各少 6 dB；它等于 γ = 2 的简化模型，城区 γ = 3.5 时 1 km 处要再低约 45 dB，所以题目说简化模型就绝不能用自由空间。
◆ 本题知识点
考什么
自由空间（Friis）公式及其 d⁻²、f⁻² 规律；和简化路径损耗模型的区别（第 2 章 p.3–4、p.8）。练习和历年卷没直接考过，但老师提醒过：往年 Quiz 有学生把简化模型的题用成了自由空间模型。
前置知识
Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)²；λ = c/f；1 W = 30 dBm；简化模型 Pr,dB = Pt,dB + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀)。
解题套路
(a) 先求 λ，dB 形式：Pr = Pt + 20 log₁₀(λ/4πd)；(b) 平方关系 → 每加倍一次 6 dB；(c) 代简化模型，和 (a) 比较。
关键数字
900 MHz、1 km：路径损耗 91.5 dB；γ = 2 时每 10 倍距离少 20 dB，γ = 3.5 时少 35 dB；1 km 处两模型差约 45 dB。
易错
题目写 simplified path loss model 就不能用自由空间；W 要换成 dBm；20 log₁₀ 而不是 10 log₁₀（λ/4πd 是幅度比，平方后才是功率比）。
直觉
自由空间是"什么都不挡"的理想情况，功率只按球面扩散减少；真实城区有遮挡和反射，衰减快得多。
联系
Part A 第五章（n = 2）；专项 Q2 双径（n = 4）；模拟 A · Q2（简化模型）。

Approach: Use the free-space formula \(P_r=P_tG_tG_r\big(\frac{\lambda}{4\pi d}\big)^2\) in dB; distance and frequency both appear squared, so each doubling costs 6 dB; in (c) compute with the simplified model and compare: the difference is tens of dB.

  1. (a) \(\lambda=c/f=3\times10^8/9\times10^8=0.333\) m, \(P_t\) = 1 W = 30 dBm.
    Pr = 30 + 20 log₁₀(0.333 / (4π × 1000)) = 30 + 20 log₁₀(2.65×10⁻⁵) = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm
    Path loss = 30 − (−61.5) = 91.5 dB (check with Part A's 32.44 + 20 log₁₀900 + 20 log₁₀1 = 91.5 dB).
  2. (b) \(P_r\propto d^{-2}\): doubling the distance costs 20 log₁₀2 = 6.0 dB (−67.5 dBm). \(P_r\propto\lambda^2\propto f^{-2}\): doubling the frequency costs another 6.0 dB (−73.6 dBm).
  3. (c) Simplified model:
    Pr = 30 + (−31.5) − 10 × 3.5 × log₁₀1000 = 30 − 31.5 − 105 = −106.5 dBm
  4. That is about 45 dB (over 30 000 times) below the free-space −61.5 dBm. In a city, buildings block and reflect the signal and the power falls much faster with distance (γ = 3.5 instead of 2); the free-space model would grossly overestimate the received power and get the coverage and outage completely wrong. When a question says "simplified path loss model", use it.
(a) Pr ≈ −61.5 dBm, path loss ≈ 91.5 dB; (b) 6 dB each (−67.5 dBm, then −73.6 dBm); (c) −106.5 dBm, about 45 dB below free space, so free space would grossly overestimate
Official answer: none (self-written practice question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
A coincidence: at 900 MHz the free-space gain at 1 m, 20 log₁₀(λ/4π) = −31.5 dB, equals the KdB used in (c). So both models agree at 1 m and differ only in the exponent: per decade of distance, free space loses 20 dB while γ = 3.5 loses 35 dB. The lecturer warned that in a past quiz some students used the free-space model on a simplified-model question.
Concept + step by step: free-space formula vs simplified path loss model

ConceptThe free-space (Friis) formula: what each symbol means

Ch 2 p.4: with no obstruction and only a direct line-of-sight (LOS) path, \(P_r=\dfrac{P_tG_tG_r\lambda^2}{(4\pi)^2d^2}\). Pt and Pr are the transmit and received powers; Gt and Gr the antenna gains (both 1 here); λ = c/f the wavelength; d the distance. The two conclusions on the slide: the received power is inversely proportional to d² and proportional to λ² (i.e. inversely proportional to f²).

The dB form is easiest to compute: Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + Gt,dB + Gr,dB + 20 log₁₀(λ/(4πd)). Why 20: taking 10 log₁₀ of (λ/4πd)² equals 20 log₁₀(λ/4πd). The Part A slides (p.51) give the practical form L(dB) = 32 + 20 log f(MHz) + 20 log d(km); the more precise constant is 32.44.

Beyond the slidesWhere d² and λ² come from (the slide does not explain why)

The factor d² comes from the power spreading over a sphere of radius d with area 4πd²; the factor λ² comes from the effective area of the receive antenna being proportional to λ², so at a higher frequency an antenna of the same gain "catches" less power.

ConceptThe simplified path loss model: free space is a special case

Slide p.8: Pr,dB = Pt,dB + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀), where γ is the path loss exponent and K and γ are fitted to measured data. The lecturer said both the linear and dB forms must be memorised (not given in the exam); "simplified path loss model" is a proper name, and in past Quizzes some students used the free-space model instead.

Free space written in the same form: with d₀ = 1 m, KdB = 20 log₁₀(λ/4π) and γ = 2. So the real difference between the two models is the exponent: per factor of 10 in distance, free space loses 20 dB, while γ = 3.5 loses 35 dB.

Step by step(a) Free-space received power at 1 km

  1. Wavelength:
    λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / (9 × 10⁸) = 0.3333 m
  2. Transmit power in dBm (the lecturer said a missing W / mW conversion loses marks):
    Pt = 1 W = 1000 mW → 10 log₁₀1000 = 30 dBm
  3. Into the dB formula (d in metres, the same unit as λ):
    λ/(4πd) = 0.3333 / (4π × 1000) = 0.3333 / 12566 = 2.65 × 10⁻⁵
    20 log₁₀(2.65 × 10⁻⁵) = 20 × (−4.576) = −91.5 dB
    Pr = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm; path loss = 30 − (−61.5) = 91.5 dB
  4. Check: 32.44 + 20 log₁₀900 + 20 log₁₀1 = 32.44 + 59.08 + 0 = 91.5 dB (here f in MHz, d in km).

Step by step(b) Doubling the distance, doubling the frequency

Pr ∝ d⁻²: doubling d → 20 log₁₀2 = 6.02 dB less → −61.53 − 6.02 = −67.55 dBm
Pr ∝ λ² ∝ f⁻²: then doubling f → another 6.02 dB less → −67.55 − 6.02 = −73.6 dBm

Rule: if a quantity appears to the power n, doubling it changes the power by n × 3 dB (10 log₁₀2 ≈ 3). In free space d and f both appear to the power −2, so each gives −6 dB.

Note: the −67.6 dBm in the solution above is a rounding slip. The exact value is −67.547 dBm, which rounds to −67.5 dBm to one decimal (−67.55 to two); the 0.05 dB difference does not change any conclusion.

Step by step(c) The simplified model, and why free space must not be used

Pr = 30 + (−31.5) − 10 × 3.5 × log₁₀1000 = 30 − 31.5 − 105 = −106.5 dBm
Difference from free space: −61.5 − (−106.5) = 45 dB (a power ratio of about 31 thousand)

Why almost exactly 45 dB: at 900 MHz the free-space KdB = 20 log₁₀(0.3333/4π) = −31.5 dB, almost the same as the KdB in the question, so the two models agree at 1 m; from 1 m to 1 km is three factors of 10, so free space loses 20 × 3 = 60 dB and the simplified model 35 × 3 = 105 dB, a difference of 45 dB.

Why it is a serious mistake: in a city, buildings block the signal and the ground and walls reflect it, so the power decays with distance much faster than in free space. Free space would overestimate the received power by about 45 dB, and coverage, outage probability and capacity would all be wrong. The question says simplified path loss model, so it must be used. Also, the table on slide p.9 gives γ = 3.7–6.5 for urban macrocells (the γ table need not be memorised), so the real decay may be even faster than γ = 3.5 and the gap even larger.

PitfallsWhere marks are usually lost

  • Using 10 log₁₀(λ/4πd): λ/4πd is an amplitude ratio, so use 20 log₁₀ (or square first, then 10 log₁₀).
  • Not converting 1 W to 30 dBm and writing Pr = 1 − 91.5.
  • Putting d in km into Friis while λ is in metres: inconsistent units. In the simplified model with d₀ = 1 m, d must also be in metres: log₁₀1000 = 3, not log₁₀1 = 0 (the lecturer said some students lost the calculation marks this way in this year's Quiz).
  • In (b), thinking the power increases when the frequency doubles: a higher f means a smaller λ and a smaller received power.

ExamHow to write it in English

(a) λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / 9 × 10⁸ = 0.333 m and Pt = 1 W = 30 dBm. In free space, Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)², so Pr,dBm = 30 + 20 log₁₀(0.333/(4π × 1000)) = 30 − 91.5 = −61.5 dBm, and the path loss is 91.5 dB.

(b) Pr ∝ d⁻², so doubling d reduces Pr by 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB (to −67.55 dBm). Pr ∝ λ² ∝ f⁻², so doubling f reduces it by another 6 dB (to −73.6 dBm).

(c) Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀) = 30 − 31.5 − 35 log₁₀(1000) = −106.5 dBm, about 45 dB below the free-space value. Free space assumes an unobstructed LOS path with γ = 2, whereas in an urban macrocell the power decays much faster (γ = 3.5) because of obstruction and reflections. Using free space would overestimate the received power by about 45 dB and give completely wrong coverage and outage results.

In one line: free space is Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)², computed with 20 log₁₀, and doubling d or f costs 6 dB each; it is the simplified model with γ = 2, and with an urban γ = 3.5 the power at 1 km is about 45 dB lower, so when the question says simplified model, never use free space.
◆ Key points
What it tests
The free-space (Friis) formula and its d⁻², f⁻² behaviour; how it differs from the simplified path loss model (Ch 2 p.3–4, p.8). Not asked directly in the practices or past papers, but the lecturer warned that past quiz students used free space on a simplified-model question.
Background
Pr = PtGtGr(λ/4πd)²; λ = c/f; 1 W = 30 dBm; simplified model Pr,dB = Pt,dB + KdB − 10γ log₁₀(d/d₀).
Method
(a) Find λ, then in dB Pr = Pt + 20 log₁₀(λ/4πd); (b) squared dependence → 6 dB per doubling; (c) use the simplified model and compare with (a).
Key numbers
900 MHz, 1 km: path loss 91.5 dB; per decade of distance γ = 2 loses 20 dB, γ = 3.5 loses 35 dB; at 1 km the two models differ by about 45 dB.
Common mistakes
If the question says simplified path loss model, do not use free space; convert W to dBm; 20 log₁₀, not 10 log₁₀ (λ/4πd is an amplitude ratio, its square is the power ratio).
Intuition
Free space is the ideal "nothing in the way" case where power only spreads over a sphere; real cities block and reflect, so the power falls much faster.
Link
Part A Ch 5 (n = 2); focused Q2 two-ray (n = 4); mock A·Q2 (simplified model).
专项 · Q2

双径模型：临界距离和 d⁻⁴

Original question · 中英对照Focused practice (self-written)

A base station antenna at height \(h_t=30\) m transmits 10 W at 900 MHz to a mobile antenna at height \(h_r=1.5\) m. Both antennas have unity gain. Use the two-ray model \(P_r\approx P_tG_tG_r\dfrac{(h_th_r)^2}{d^4}\) for \(d>d_c=\dfrac{4h_th_r}{\lambda}\).

基站天线高 \(h_t=30\) m，在 900 MHz 上发射 10 W，移动台天线高 \(h_r=1.5\) m，两根天线增益都是 1。当 \(d>d_c=\dfrac{4h_th_r}{\lambda}\) 时使用双径模型 \(P_r\approx P_tG_tG_r\dfrac{(h_th_r)^2}{d^4}\)。

(a) Find the critical distance \(d_c\). (2 marks)

(a) 求临界距离 \(d_c\)。（2 分）

(b) Find the received power at d = 5 km with the two-ray model, and compare it with the free-space value at the same distance. (4 marks)

(b) 用双径模型求 d = 5 km 处的接收功率，并和同距离的自由空间值比较。（4 分）

(c) Beyond \(d_c\), how does the two-ray received power change when (i) the distance is doubled, (ii) the carrier frequency is doubled, (iii) the base station antenna height is doubled? (4 marks)

(c) 在 \(d_c\) 以外，以下情况双径接收功率怎么变化：(i) 距离加倍；(ii) 载频加倍；(iii) 基站天线高度加倍？（4 分）

双径模型临界距离 d_cP ∝ d⁻⁴讲义第 2 章 p.5
题意讲解：这道题到底要干嘛

场景题目在讲一件什么事

讲义第 2 章 p.5 的双径模型：除了直射波，还有一条从地面反射过来的波。离得远时，两条波几乎反相，大部分互相抵消，所以功率比自由空间掉得快，按 d⁻⁴ 减少。

题目让你算出 d⁻⁴ 公式开始适用的临界距离，用它算远处的功率，再看距离、频率、天线高度变化时功率怎么变。

符号题目里每个量是什么

\(h_t=30\) m、\(h_r=1.5\) m基站和手机的天线高度
\(d_c=4h_th_r/\lambda\)临界距离：比它远，才按 d⁻⁴ 单调下降
\(P_r\approx P_tG_tG_r(h_th_r)^2/d^4\)远处的双径公式，里面没有 λ
10 W、900 MHz发射功率和载频

要求每一问在问什么

  • (a) 代公式求临界距离。
  • (b) 5 km 比临界距离远得多，用双径公式算接收功率，再和同距离的自由空间结果比较。
  • (c) 看 d、λ、ht 在公式里分别是几次方：距离加倍、频率加倍、天线加高一倍，功率各变多少 dB。

注意读题容易误会的地方

  • d 小于 dc 时不能用 d⁻⁴ 公式。
  • 远处的双径公式里没有 λ，所以频率变了，功率不变。

目的这道题想让你明白什么

双径是 γ = 4 的例子，自由空间是 γ = 2 的例子；真实环境的 γ 常在两者之间，这就是简化模型里 γ 要靠实测拟合的原因。

一句话：算双径模型的临界距离，用 d⁻⁴ 公式求 5 km 的功率并和自由空间比，再看距离、频率、天线高度加倍的影响。

思路：先由 λ 算临界距离；d 大于它时直接代双径公式（注意里面没有 λ）；(c) 看每个量在公式里的幂次，换成 dB。

  1. (a) \(\lambda=0.333\) m：
    d_c = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.333 = 540 m
  2. (b) 5 km 远大于 540 m，可以用双径公式：
    P_r = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 10 × 2025 / 6.25×10¹⁴ = 3.24×10⁻¹¹ W = −74.9 dBm
  3. 同距离的自由空间：\(P_r=10\times\big(\frac{0.333}{4\pi\times5000}\big)^2=2.81\times10^{-10}\) W = −65.5 dBm。双径低了约 9.4 dB：远处地面反射和直射几乎反相抵消，所以比自由空间衰减得快。
  4. (c)(i) \(P_r\propto d^{-4}\)：距离加倍，少 40 log₁₀2 = 12 dB（自由空间只少 6 dB）。(ii) 公式里没有 λ：频率加倍，不变（自由空间会少 6 dB）。(iii) \(P_r\propto h_t^2\)：天线高度加倍，多 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB。
(a) d_c = 540 m；(b) −74.9 dBm，比自由空间（−65.5 dBm）低约 9.4 dB；(c) 距离加倍 −12 dB，频率加倍不变，天线高度加倍 +6 dB
官方答案：无（自编模拟题）。所有数字已用 src/p2build/verify_mock.py 验算。
d < dc 时直射和反射时而相加、时而抵消，功率随距离上下起伏，不能用 d⁻⁴ 公式；d > dc 才单调按 d⁻⁴ 下降。这就是 Part A 第五章说的 n = 2（自由空间）和 n = 4（双径远处），也是简化模型里 γ 常在 2 到 4 之间的原因之一。
知识讲解 + 详细解析：双径模型、临界距离与幂次换 dB

知识讲解双径模型：直射 + 一次地面反射

讲义第 2 章 p.5：接收端收到两路信号，一路直射（LOS），一路经地面反射一次。超过临界距离（critical distance）dc 后，接收功率近似为 \(P_r\approx P_tG_tG_r\dfrac{(h_th_r)^2}{d^4}\)，\(d_c=\dfrac{4h_th_r}{\lambda}\)。ht、hr 是收发天线高度，λ 是波长。Part A 第五章把它说成"远处 n = 4"，自由空间是 n = 2。

取 dB 形式：Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + Gt,dB + Gr,dB + 20 log₁₀(hthr) − 40 log₁₀d。注意：远处的公式里没有 λ，λ 只出现在 dc 里。

课外补充为什么远处是 d⁻⁴（讲义只给结论）

距离很远时，反射路径只比直射路径长约 2hthr/d，而且地面反射会把相位翻转约 180°，两路几乎等大反相、大部分抵消。剩下的差值幅度和 1/d² 成正比，功率就和 1/d⁴ 成正比。d < dc 时两路时而相加、时而抵消，功率随距离上下起伏，d⁻⁴ 公式不适用。

详细解析(a) 临界距离

λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / (9 × 10⁸) = 0.3333 m
dc = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.3333 = 180 / 0.3333 = 540 m

为什么先算它：双径的 d⁻⁴ 公式只在 d > dc 时成立，(b)(c) 都要先确认距离在 dc 以外。

详细解析(b) 5 km 处：双径对比自由空间

  1. 检查条件：5000 m > 540 m，可以用双径公式。
  2. 线性算：
    Pr = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 10 × 2025 / 6.25 × 10¹⁴ = 3.24 × 10⁻¹¹ W
  3. 换 dBm（10 W = 40 dBm），也可以直接用 dB 形式核对：
    10 log₁₀(3.24 × 10⁻¹¹) + 30 = −74.9 dBm
    dB 形式：40 + 20 log₁₀45 − 40 log₁₀5000 = 40 + 33.06 − 147.96 = −74.9 dBm
  4. 同距离自由空间：
    Pr = 40 + 20 log₁₀(0.3333 / (4π × 5000)) = 40 − 105.5 = −65.5 dBm
    双径比自由空间低：−65.5 − (−74.9) = 9.4 dB

课外补充差值的一般式（由讲义的两个公式自己相除得到）

双径 ÷ 自由空间 = (4πhthr/(λd))² = (πdc/d)²，所以差值 = 20 log₁₀(d/(πdc)) = 20 log₁₀(5000/1696) = 9.4 dB。π dc ≈ 1.7 km 以外双径比自由空间低，而且距离每远 10 倍，多低 20 dB。

详细解析(c) 看幂次，换成 dB

方法：某个量在公式里是 n 次方，它加倍时功率变化 10 log₁₀(2ⁿ) = n × 3.01 dB。

变化双径（d > dc）自由空间
(i) d 加倍d⁻⁴ → −40 log₁₀2 = −12.04 dBd⁻² → −6.02 dB
(ii) f 加倍公式里没有 λ → 不变（0 dB）λ² → −6.02 dB
(iii) ht 加倍ht² → +20 log₁₀2 = +6.02 dB和高度无关 → 0 dB

别忘了检查前提：(ii) 频率加倍时 λ 减半，dc 变成 1080 m；(iii) ht 加倍时 dc 也变成 1080 m。两种情况 5 km 仍在 dc 以外，结论成立。

直觉：把基站天线架高，两条路径的长度差变大，抵消得少一些，收到的功率就多；这也是基站天线要装得高的原因之一。

易错常见丢分点

  • 没先检查 d > dc 就用 d⁻⁴ 公式。
  • 以为双径远处功率也和频率有关：频率只影响 dc。
  • dB 系数用错：d⁻⁴ 是 −40 log₁₀d，(hthr)² 是 +20 log₁₀(hthr)。
  • 10 W 没换成 40 dBm；或者 W 换 dBm 时忘了加 30。
  • 算 dc 时 λ 用错（900 MHz 对应 1/3 m）。

考试英文写法

(a) λ = c/f = 0.333 m, so dc = 4hthr/λ = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.333 = 540 m.

(b) Since d = 5 km > dc, the two-ray approximation applies: Pr = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 3.24 × 10⁻¹¹ W = −74.9 dBm. In free space, Pr = 10 × (0.333/(4π × 5000))² = 2.81 × 10⁻¹⁰ W = −65.5 dBm. The two-ray power is about 9.4 dB lower, because far from the transmitter the ground-reflected ray nearly cancels the direct ray.

(c) Beyond dc, Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴ and does not depend on λ. (i) Doubling d reduces Pr by 40 log₁₀2 = 12 dB. (ii) Doubling the carrier frequency leaves Pr unchanged (only dc doubles to 1080 m, still below 5 km). (iii) Doubling ht increases Pr by 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB.

一句话：先算 dc = 4hthr/λ 并确认 d > dc；远处 Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴ 与频率无关；看幂次换 dB：距离加倍 −12 dB，频率加倍不变，天线高度加倍 +6 dB。
◆ 本题知识点
考什么
双径模型：临界距离 dc = 4hthr/λ，远处 Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴（第 2 章 p.5）。练习和历年卷没直接考过。
前置知识
λ = c/f；功率 dB = 10 log₁₀(W) + 30 dBm；幂次换 dB：x 的 n 次方，x 加倍就是 n × 3 dB。
解题套路
(a) 代 dc；(b) 确认 d > dc 后代双径公式，再和自由空间比；(c) 看 d、λ、ht 在公式里的幂次：d⁻⁴ → −12 dB，λ⁰ → 不变，ht² → +6 dB。
关键数字
dc = 540 m；5 km 处双径 −74.9 dBm，自由空间 −65.5 dBm，差 9.4 dB。
易错
d < dc 时不能用 d⁻⁴ 公式；双径远处的公式里没有 λ，频率变了功率不变；天线高度是平方关系。
直觉
远处地面反射波和直射波几乎反相，大部分抵消，只剩一点差值，所以比自由空间衰减得快；把天线架高可以拉大两条路径的差，收到的功率就多一些。
联系
Part A 第五章（n = 4）；专项 Q1 自由空间（n = 2）；讲解页 2.1 节的推导框。

Approach: Find the critical distance from λ; beyond it, use the two-ray formula directly (note that it contains no λ); for (c), read off the power of each quantity in the formula and convert to dB.

  1. (a) \(\lambda=0.333\) m:
    dc = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.333 = 540 m
  2. (b) 5 km is far beyond 540 m, so the two-ray formula applies:
    Pr = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 10 × 2025 / 6.25×10¹⁴ = 3.24×10⁻¹¹ W = −74.9 dBm
  3. Free space at the same distance: \(P_r=10\times\big(\frac{0.333}{4\pi\times5000}\big)^2=2.81\times10^{-10}\) W = −65.5 dBm. The two-ray value is about 9.4 dB lower: far away the ground reflection nearly cancels the direct wave, so the power falls faster than in free space.
  4. (c)(i) \(P_r\propto d^{-4}\): doubling the distance costs 40 log₁₀2 = 12 dB (free space only 6 dB). (ii) There is no λ in the formula: doubling the frequency changes nothing (free space would lose 6 dB). (iii) \(P_r\propto h_t^2\): doubling the antenna height gains 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB.
(a) dc = 540 m; (b) −74.9 dBm, about 9.4 dB below free space (−65.5 dBm); (c) doubling distance −12 dB, doubling frequency no change, doubling antenna height +6 dB
Official answer: none (self-written practice question); all numbers checked by src/p2build/verify_mock.py
For d < dc the direct and reflected waves alternately add and cancel, so the power rises and falls with distance and the d⁻⁴ formula does not apply; only beyond dc does it fall steadily as d⁻⁴. This is the n = 2 (free space) and n = 4 (two-ray, far) of Part A Ch 5, and one reason why γ in the simplified model usually lies between 2 and 4.
Concept + step by step: two-ray model, critical distance, powers to dB

ConceptThe two-ray model: direct ray + one ground reflection

Ch 2 p.5: the receiver gets two signals, one direct (LOS) and one reflected once by the ground. Beyond the critical distance dc, the received power is approximately \(P_r\approx P_tG_tG_r\dfrac{(h_th_r)^2}{d^4}\), with \(d_c=\dfrac{4h_th_r}{\lambda}\). ht and hr are the antenna heights and λ the wavelength. Part A chapter 5 calls this "n = 4 at large distance"; free space is n = 2.

In dB: Pr,dBm = Pt,dBm + Gt,dB + Gr,dB + 20 log₁₀(hthr) − 40 log₁₀d. Note: there is no λ in the far-distance formula; λ appears only in dc.

Beyond the slidesWhy it is d⁻⁴ far away (the slide gives only the result)

At a large distance the reflected path is only about 2hthr/d longer than the direct path, and the ground reflection flips the phase by about 180°, so the two rays are almost equal and opposite and mostly cancel. The amplitude of what is left is proportional to 1/d², so the power is proportional to 1/d⁴. For d < dc the two rays alternately add and cancel, the power oscillates with distance, and the d⁻⁴ formula does not apply.

Step by step(a) Critical distance

λ = c/f = 3 × 10⁸ / (9 × 10⁸) = 0.3333 m
dc = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.3333 = 180 / 0.3333 = 540 m

Why compute it first: the two-ray d⁻⁴ formula holds only for d > dc, so (b) and (c) both need to confirm the distance is beyond dc.

Step by step(b) At 5 km: two-ray vs free space

  1. Check the condition: 5000 m > 540 m, so the two-ray formula can be used.
  2. Linear:
    Pr = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 10 × 2025 / 6.25 × 10¹⁴ = 3.24 × 10⁻¹¹ W
  3. To dBm (10 W = 40 dBm), or check directly with the dB form:
    10 log₁₀(3.24 × 10⁻¹¹) + 30 = −74.9 dBm
    dB form: 40 + 20 log₁₀45 − 40 log₁₀5000 = 40 + 33.06 − 147.96 = −74.9 dBm
  4. Free space at the same distance:
    Pr = 40 + 20 log₁₀(0.3333 / (4π × 5000)) = 40 − 105.5 = −65.5 dBm
    two-ray is lower than free space by −65.5 − (−74.9) = 9.4 dB

Beyond the slidesThe general gap (obtained by dividing the two slide formulas yourself)

Two-ray ÷ free space = (4πhthr/(λd))² = (πdc/d)², so the difference = 20 log₁₀(d/(πdc)) = 20 log₁₀(5000/1696) = 9.4 dB. Beyond π dc ≈ 1.7 km the two-ray power is below free space, and every factor of 10 in distance adds another 20 dB to the gap.

Step by step(c) Read off the powers, convert to dB

Method: if a quantity appears to the power n, doubling it changes the power by 10 log₁₀(2ⁿ) = n × 3.01 dB.

ChangeTwo-ray (d > dc)Free space
(i) d doubledd⁻⁴ → −40 log₁₀2 = −12.04 dBd⁻² → −6.02 dB
(ii) f doubledno λ in the formula → unchanged (0 dB)λ² → −6.02 dB
(iii) ht doubledht² → +20 log₁₀2 = +6.02 dBindependent of height → 0 dB

Do not forget to check the condition: in (ii) doubling the frequency halves λ, so dc becomes 1080 m; in (iii) doubling ht also makes dc 1080 m. In both cases 5 km is still beyond dc, so the conclusions hold.

Intuition: raising the base station antenna increases the path-length difference between the two rays, so they cancel less and more power arrives; this is one reason base station antennas are mounted high.

PitfallsWhere marks are usually lost

  • Using the d⁻⁴ formula without first checking d > dc.
  • Thinking the far-distance two-ray power depends on frequency: the frequency only affects dc.
  • Wrong dB coefficients: d⁻⁴ is −40 log₁₀d, (hthr)² is +20 log₁₀(hthr).
  • Not converting 10 W to 40 dBm, or forgetting the +30 when converting W to dBm.
  • Using the wrong λ for dc (900 MHz corresponds to 1/3 m).

ExamHow to write it in English

(a) λ = c/f = 0.333 m, so dc = 4hthr/λ = 4 × 30 × 1.5 / 0.333 = 540 m.

(b) Since d = 5 km > dc, the two-ray approximation applies: Pr = 10 × (30 × 1.5)² / 5000⁴ = 3.24 × 10⁻¹¹ W = −74.9 dBm. In free space, Pr = 10 × (0.333/(4π × 5000))² = 2.81 × 10⁻¹⁰ W = −65.5 dBm. The two-ray power is about 9.4 dB lower, because far from the transmitter the ground-reflected ray nearly cancels the direct ray.

(c) Beyond dc, Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴ and does not depend on λ. (i) Doubling d reduces Pr by 40 log₁₀2 = 12 dB. (ii) Doubling the carrier frequency leaves Pr unchanged (only dc doubles to 1080 m, still below 5 km). (iii) Doubling ht increases Pr by 20 log₁₀2 = 6 dB.

In one line: first compute dc = 4hthr/λ and confirm d > dc; far away Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴, independent of frequency; read off the powers in dB: doubling distance −12 dB, doubling frequency no change, doubling antenna height +6 dB.
◆ Key points
What it tests
The two-ray model: critical distance dc = 4hthr/λ and Pr ∝ (hthr)²/d⁴ far away (Ch 2 p.5). Not asked directly in the practices or past papers.
Background
λ = c/f; power in dBm = 10 log₁₀(W) + 30; powers to dB: if a quantity appears to the n-th power, doubling it changes the result by n × 3 dB.
Method
(a) Substitute into dc; (b) check d > dc, use the two-ray formula, then compare with free space; (c) read the powers of d, λ and ht: d⁻⁴ → −12 dB, λ⁰ → no change, ht² → +6 dB.
Key numbers
dc = 540 m; at 5 km two-ray −74.9 dBm, free space −65.5 dBm, a 9.4 dB difference.
Common mistakes
The d⁻⁴ formula is not valid for d < dc; the far two-ray formula has no λ, so the power does not change with frequency; antenna height enters squared.
Intuition
Far away the ground reflection is almost opposite in phase to the direct wave and cancels most of it, leaving a small difference, so the power falls faster than in free space; raising the antenna increases the path difference and recovers some power.
Link
Part A Ch 5 (n = 4); focused Q1 free space (n = 2); the derivation box in section 2.1 of the learning page.
专项 · Q3

一个场景里的六种信道效应：路径损耗、阴影、瑞利、莱斯、平坦/选择性、快/慢

Original question · 中英对照Focused practice (self-written)

A mobile station is 1 km from a base station that transmits 1 W. The simplified path loss model applies with \(K_{dB}=-40\) dB, \(d_0=1\) m and γ = 3. The noise power at the receiver is −120 dBm.
